Together with robustness to image perturbations and variation in authentic-image sources, these results support authentic-image regularities as a transferable basis for detection across diverse generators and image conditions.

new PhysForce: Physics-aware Force Control for Interactive Video World Model

Authors: Xuancheng Xu, Xiaofeng Wang, Yue Ma, Zheng Zhu, Bing-Kun Bao

Abstract: Interactive video world models aim to predict how visual environments evolve in response to user interventions, including physically consistent state changes induced by applied forces. Existing methods often represent forces as motion trajectories, which may introduce inappropriate motion priors, causing the model to over-rely on encoded displacement patterns. More fundamentally, the model's internal representations do not reliably localize force-induced state changes, making it difficult to associate applied forces with their resulting physical responses. Also, limited force-annotated data make it difficult to produce physically consistent and temporally stable predictions in unseen scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose PhysForce, a two-stage framework that integrates supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning through three complementary components. During supervised fine-tuning, (1) the Latent Force Intervention Encoder maps force location, direction, and magnitude into the video latent space without exposing future motion; and (2) the Physics Attribution Network associates force interventions with localized changes in the latent state, strengthening the learning of physical responses to applied forces. During reinforcement learning, (3) Physics-Aware Reinforcement Learning uses self-supervised rewards on out-of-distribution videos to improve physical consistency and generalization. Extensive experiments show that PhysForce improves force responsiveness, physical consistency, and generalization, demonstrating its effectiveness for interactive video world models.

new Mem-X: A Memory-Dependent Cross-Episode Benchmark for Vision-and-Language Navigation

Authors: Zhikang Shi, Yuk Tung Samuel Fang, Hongyu Ding, Zixuan Chen, Yujin Wang, Junhui Guo, You Wu, Jieqi Shi, Jing Huo, Yang Gao

Abstract: Long-term memory enables embodied agents to reuse past observations across tasks. However, existing cross-episode vision-and-language navigation (VLN) benchmarks often leave it unclear whether successful navigation relies on historical memory or current exploration. We introduce Mem-X, a benchmark designed to evaluate memory-dependent cross-episode VLN. Mem-X organizes episodes into sessions in which subsequent instructions refer to objects or routes encountered in earlier episodes. Its three levels evaluate historical-object recall, in-memory localization and episodic indexing, and off-memory relocalization and historical-route reproduction. For the latter two levels, agents receive episode-local poses but no transformations between episode frames, requiring them to establish spatial correspondence with historical memory. Mem-X comprises 2,071 episodes across 710 sessions in 34 scenes, including 1,204 memory-dependent episodes. We also provide MemBridge, a modular baseline that combines semantic scene memory, cross-episode localization, and coarse-to-fine retrieval to support existing VLN agents. Experiments with state-of-the-art VLN methods demonstrate the benefits of MemBridge while revealing substantial room for improvement on memory-dependent navigation. The benchmark and baseline code are available at https://github.com/NJU-R-L-Group-Embodied-Lab/Mem-X.

URLs: https://github.com/NJU-R-L-Group-Embodied-Lab/Mem-X.

new FedQCal: Quadratic Programming with Dual-Space Calibration for One-Shot Federated Learning

Authors: Shunsei Koshibuchi, Hiroshi Kera, Kazuhiko Kawamoto

Abstract: One-shot federated learning must combine heterogeneous client models after a single upload. Different local class distributions make it difficult to decide which client updates to retain. We propose FedQCal, which uses a reference model trained on a small labeled server set to define alignment requirements for aggregation. The key idea is to retain sufficient reference alignment while avoiding unnecessarily large aggregation coefficients. A minimum-norm quadratic program (QP) enforces these requirements. Classification loss on the same server examples selects a merge through two dual variables, so the alignment thresholds need not be prescribed in advance. We prove that the selected coefficients remain exact QP solutions at their induced thresholds. A perturbation bound explains how coefficient norm limits the amplification of projection errors. Experiments on CIFAR-10, CIFAR-100, and SVHN show gains over one-round FedAvg in most settings. Ablations support reference-guided weighting and coefficient calibration, even with one labeled server image per class.

new GEG-SAM: Gaze Exploration-Guided Segment Anything Model for Weakly Supervised Polyp Segmentation

Authors: Ao Wang, Lei Zhang, Lituan Wang, Zhenwei Zhang, Xiangbao Gong, Zhiyi Zhang, Shunyao Wang, Xianglei Yuan, Zizhou Wang

Abstract: Endoscopist gaze offers an unobtrusively acquired record of lesion-oriented visual exploration during routine clinical inspection, providing relevant supervision for tasks. However, existing methods treat fixations as isolated spatial prompts directly associated with the target, overlooking the fact that natural gaze records a continuous visual search process in which fixations are temporally connected, spatially organized, and coupled with the visual content. Interpreting each fixation independently based only on its local visual content overlooks the temporal evolution and spatial organization of the gaze trajectory, whereas modeling relations among fixations alone cannot reveal the visual semantics of the regions they attend to. To this end, we propose the Gaze Exploration-Guided Segment Anything Model (GEG-SAM), a principled weakly supervised framework designed to explicitly exploit structured gaze relations and their corresponding visual cues to modulate SAM for polyp segmentation. Specifically, GEG-SAM captures structured gaze relations by modeling the temporal dynamics and spatial topology of gaze exploration. It then associates these relational representations with fixation-aligned visual context to derive gaze-conditioned tokens, which are further injected into the frozen SAM encoder to modulate its pretrained image features. In this way, GEG-SAM effectively exploits the rich information embedded in gaze exploration to guide SAM toward lesion-oriented segmentation. Extensive experiments on five public polyp segmentation datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, improving the mean Dice by 9.30 percentage points over the strongest weakly supervised baseline.

new SCAP: Stochastic Context for Action Expert Pretraining

Authors: Xiao Lin, Yaoxu Lyu, Ronghao Dang, Zhili Liu, Kaijing Ma, Yang Zhou, Dingning Liu, Runzhe Teng, Jun Cen, Shanghang Zhang, Chengju Liu, Qijun Chen

Abstract: Single-modality action-expert pretraining controls competition from visual shortcuts before vision--language--action (VLA) adaptation. Yet single-modality pretraining can impair the vision--language interactions needed for subsequent joint prediction. We construct a diagnostic model in which VA or LA pretraining can disable an initially active pathway for joint vision--language modeling. In this model, subsequent training with complete visual and language inputs cannot restore the pathway, even with unlimited adaptation. We therefore introduce Stochastic Context for Action Expert Pretraining (SCAP), a new action-expert pretraining stage that precedes full-input training. During this stage, visual and text tokens are randomly masked before entering the VLM. In this unified diagnostic model, SCAP can preserve the joint vision--language modeling pathway during pretraining and subsequent full-input training. Compared with direct full-input training, it can also reduce reliance on visual shortcuts and increase reliance on language instructions. SCAP consistently improves performance across four different VLA architectures. In real-robot experiments, it achieves 76.67% average success across three instruction-conditioned manipulation tasks, outperforming full-input pretraining by 25 percentage points.

new DepthMSA: Combinatorial Training and Budget-Aware Depth Allocation for Multimodal Sentiment Analysis

Authors: Pan Wang, Jie Peng, Zhimin Li, Jingxian Chen, Hui Ji, Huanrui Yang, Pengfei Zhou, Tianlong Chen, Jingtong Hu

Abstract: State-of-the-art multimodal sentiment analysis (MSA) models rely on large pretrained encoders, yet typically process every modality to its full depth, regardless of its computational cost or predictive contribution. This raises a fundamental question: how should limited computation be allocated across modalities to preserve predictive performance? We reveal a substantial mismatch between the computational cost of modality encoders and the predictive benefit of processing them more deeply, suggesting that different modalities require different encoding depths. However, enabling heterogeneous depths introduces a key challenge: representations extracted at different depths must still be effectively fused. To address this, we propose $\textbf{DepthMSA}$, a budget-aware framework that supports flexible modality depths within a single MSA model. Specifically, we introduce combinatorial anytime training, which supervises the fusion head across all cross-modal depth combinations using a single full-depth encoder pass. At inference, a training-free, cost-aware allocator selects the configuration with the lowest validation-calibrated error under a sample-specific FLOPs budget. On CMU-MOSEI, DepthMSA maintains accuracy within one percentage point of the full-depth model while reducing measured FLOPs by 45\%, or by 82\% with a separately trained skip-enabled variant. When a CLIP visual encoder dominates computation, the allocation shifts toward reducing visual depth, achieving 56\% fewer FLOPs at unchanged accuracy. Compared with ADMN, an adaptive layer-allocation method that trains a controller for each budget, the skip-enabled variant reaches its accuracy with 3.2$\times$ fewer FLOPs and lower latency, without any per-budget training. Our project is available at https://pwang322.github.io/DepthMSA/.

URLs: https://pwang322.github.io/DepthMSA/.

new Backbone-Specific LoRA Geometry: Orthogonality, Superposition, and Provenance

Authors: Kazuhiko Kawamoto, Keigo Watanabe, Hiroshi Kera

Abstract: Sharing one low-rank adaptation (LoRA) update across pretrained models simplifies deployment. Composition methods typically combine updates from a common backbone, while cross-model transfer transforms updates for a target backbone. The conditions for sharing updates across backbones while preserving accuracy and distinguishing individual contributions remain unclear. Our idea is to use backbone-specific update directions as signatures of their training backbones. We derive a cross-backbone correlation bound from attention symmetries and equivariant LoRA learning under independent, uniform pretrained feature orientations. A local gradient-flow model explains positive mean correlation between independent runs on the same backbone and task. These patterns motivate cosine-based backbone identification and inner-product tests for registered-adapter reuse without coordinate alignment. Experiments on CLIP backbones show that shared sums achieve nearly the same mean accuracy as separate adapters. Cosine scores reliably identify source backbones across the evaluated tasks. Registered adapters remain detectable in sums of adapters from different backbones.

new Parameter-Efficient Adaptation of Qwen3-VL for Handwritten Mathematical Expression Recognition

Authors: Manh Tuan Ha, Xuan Bao Viet Tran, Vo Minh Dat Nguyen, Minh Nhat Vo, Quoc Trung Nguyen, Van Bay Nguyen

Abstract: Handwritten mathematical expression recognition (HMER) requires joint recovery of symbol identity and two-dimensional syntax, so a locally plausible transcription may still be structurally wrong. We study whether a compact vision-language model can be adapted to generate linearized Symbol Label Graphs (SLGs) under limited computing resources. Starting from a 4-bit Qwen3-VL-4B checkpoint, we apply rank-16 Low-Rank Adaptation to the vision, language, attention, and MLP modules and train with instruction-formatted CROHME 2019 examples. We compare this model with an internally implemented ViT-Transformer baseline, a zero-shot Qwen3-VL configuration, and additional internally evaluated alternatives. On the 1,198 valid CROHME test examples, the CROHME-only model obtains 524 exact matches (43.74%) and a corpus BLEU-4 score of 77.67, compared with 4.92% and 43.26 for the task-specific ViT baseline. An ablation that adds 2,000 manually written, formula-guided samples decreases exact match to 42.32%, exposing a domain-alignment issue rather than an automatic benefit from more data. The principal contribution is therefore an evidence-backed, resource-efficient formulation of HMER as multimodal structural generation, together with a controlled data-composition ablation and an analysis of the remaining gap between local token overlap and globally correct structure.

new EAST: Event-Anchored Surface Transport for Event-Based Gaussian Reconstruction

Authors: Ke Luo, Wei Zhai, Yuliang Wu, Pengfei Yu, Yang Cao

Abstract: Event-supervised Gaussian reconstruction combines the high temporal resolution and wide dynamic range of event cameras with efficient explicit rendering. However, recovering a continuous surface remains difficult: changes in rendered appearance can compensate for depth errors while preserving the predicted brightness changes. We introduce Event-Anchored Surface Transport (EAST), which couples an event-constrained signed distance field to Gaussian rendering. Triangulation-supported candidates anchor the zero set, while normal flow measured from event timing constrains ray-surface intersection depths. Gaussian centers receive bounded corrections from the descriptor difference between the live field and a frozen initial reference. The correction is zero at initialization, yet transmits image gradients to the live field. Experiments on PAEv3D and NeRF Synthetic show improved surface accuracy and reference-view rendering over Gaussian baselines, together with efficient mesh extraction from the learned field.

new Prune to Expand: Training Prompt Enhancers with Less to Imitate

Authors: Tianhe Wu, Qilin Sun, Kede Ma

Abstract: Prompt enhancement (PE) refines concise user prompts into detailed descriptions to improve the compositional aesthetics of generated images while preserving user intent. A common practice constructs supervised fine-tuning (SFT) pairs by deriving short, user-like prompts from dense captions, implicitly assuming that captions that improve image generation also provide effective supervision. In this paper, however, we uncover a counterintuitive finding: PE models trained on caption pairs with substantial generation gains perform comparably to those trained on pairs with only marginal gains. We argue that dense captions can impose redundant supervision by requiring PE models to reproduce image-specific details weakly related to user intent. Motivated by this discrepancy, we introduce Prune to Expand (P2E), a simple text-only method that refines dense-caption supervision by selectively removing redundant sentences under a per-example deletion bound while preserving explicit user requirements and informative visual structure. Extensive experiments under identical training configurations demonstrate that P2E improves alignment with human preferences and visual quality over conventional dense-caption SFT across diverse image categories and visual generators.

new WINSO: A Large-Size Very-High-Resolution Remote Sensing Dataset for Wind Turbine Detection and Solar Panel Segmentation

Authors: Di Wang, Ke Li, Zhaokun Li, Weiping Ni, Junzheng Wu, Yahu Liu, Quan Wang, Xinbo Gao

Abstract: Wind and solar energy are central to the global transition toward net-zero emissions and sustainable development. Accurate spatial inventories of wind and solar facilities are essential for future energy planning. Satellite remote sensing provides a practical and efficient means to acquire such geospatial data at scale. However, reliable facility mapping from large-size satellite images remains constrained by the scarcity of carefully annotated datasets and the lack of systematic evaluation protocols. To address these limitations, we introduce \textbf{WINSO}, a large-scale wind and solar benchmark based on very-high-resolution (VHR) satellite imagery with ground sampling distances (GSDs) of 0.5--0.7\,m. WINSO comprises 10,962 large-size images sampled from diverse geographic locations across the Asia-Pacific region, covering at least 71,600\,$\text{km}^2$ and totaling 286.36 gigapixels, with individual image dimensions reaching 44,032$\times$62,208 pixels. Through manual annotation and expert verification, a total of 166,473 instances are labeled using horizontal bounding boxes (HBBs) for wind turbines and polygon masks for solar panels. To build baselines for clean-energy facility mapping, we evaluate state-of-the-art detection and segmentation models, specialized ultra-high-resolution (UHR) architectures, and vision-language models across different inference strategies. Furthermore, cross-dataset evaluations on two external public datasets illustrate the strong generalization capability of the WINSO. WINSO establishes a standardized benchmark to advance automated clean-energy mapping, paving the way for wide-area monitoring of the clean-energy transition.

new Beyond Prompt Imitation: Capability-Aware Video Generation with Smaller Models

Authors: Ziqi Ding, Yuxin Cao, Kai Ye, Yaobo Yang, Wei Song

Abstract: Inference-time methods that lift a small open video generator toward a proprietary one are compared through preference scores, which never say how much of the gap to a target video they close. We introduce the oracle control ladder, which hands a fixed student progressively richer information from a target clip and reads the closure of each fidelity metric at each rung as an empirical headroom reference for the methods that must synthesize that information. On 954 clips of the principal student, a dense caption reproduces little of the appearance and almost none of the motion of a target, a first frame closes half of the appearance gap and a tenth of the motion gap, a depth video closes three quarters of the motion gap and leaves color untouched, and the two together close 64 to 94% of every fidelity gap across four teachers, while a second student agrees on the rungs that it accepts. An item response model forecasts which rung closes which clip with held-out AUC of 0.76 to 0.95 on the fidelity criteria and serves as the prior of a budgeted selection policy. When controls are free, a fixed dense-control rung is hard to improve upon, and once controls are priced the policy lies above the fixed-rung frontier at matched cost and survives critics that ruin critic-driven search.

new EAGLE: Entity-Aware Grounding with Layered Evidence for High-Resolution Image Understanding

Authors: Zhongjian Zou, Quan Qian

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) still struggle with fine-grained understanding of high-resolution images: attention heads vary in their reliability for grounding target entities and their attributes, while localized details are often disconnected from the broader scene context. We propose EAGLE (Entity-Aware Grounding with Layered Evidence for High-Resolution Image Understanding), a plug-in framework that jointly selects grounding signals and organizes hierarchical visual evidence. EAGLE employs a fine-grained dataset to screen visually-grounded attention heads, followed by entity-aware grounding to extract target-specific regions. It then integrates hierarchical evidence construction and question-guided routing to select optimal view combinations, feeding them into the frozen MLLM to enhance fine-grained visual understanding. Experiments with Qwen2.5-VL-7B and InternVL3-8B demonstrate that EAGLE consistently improves performance across both backbones and achieves SOTA results on V*, HRBench-4K, and HRBench-8K compared with existing methods using the same backbone.

new PP-CFG: Polarity-Preserving Guidance for Controllable Few-Step Image and Video Generation

Authors: Junyi He, Xu Peng, Chengming Xu, Sixiao Zheng, Donghao Luo, Chao Wen, Peng Tang, Xijie Huang, Chengjie Wang, Yanwei Fu

Abstract: Few-step diffusion distillation reduces sampling cost but often weakens adjustable classifier-free guidance (CFG) and runtime negative prompting. We introduce PP-CFG, an architecture that keeps positive and negative text conditions separate and combines their attention responses with opposite signs within a shared visual backbone. Its Scale-Time Polarity Mixer refines each condition using the guidance scale and denoising timestep, allowing prompt influence to vary throughout generation. The student retains a continuous scale input and selectable negative prompts with one visual backbone pass per step. We evaluate PP-CFG on Wan2.1 for video generation with distribution matching distillation (DMD), and on Qwen-Image with both latent consistency model (LCM) distillation and DMD. PP-CFG achieves 81.46 VBench in four steps and lower video teacher-student distances across guidance scales than step-distilled DMD. On NegElement-1K, our benchmark of 1,000 matched image and video prompt cases, it achieves higher target-removal rates than the corresponding teachers. These experiments support the method's generality across attention architectures, generation modalities, and distillation objectives while retaining competitive generation quality.

new Budgeted Safety Verification for Streaming Video Generation

Authors: Yuxin Cao, Ziqi Ding, Jiayu Zhang, Xianglin Yang, Wei Song, Jin Song Dong

Abstract: A streamed video generator releases each chunk the moment that it is produced, so a safety decision that waits for the finished clip arrives after the harm has been seen. We measure a guardrail by exposure, the harmful frames a viewer is shown, and ask where a fixed moderation budget should be spent. The accurate detector reads decoded frames with a video language model and is too expensive for every chunk, so our guard reads hidden states the generator has computed already and spends a cheap score on deciding when to call it. On 599 streams from a distilled causal generator the undefended model shows 46.25 harmful frames of 81. The video model on every chunk leaves 0.91 frames at 80.1 scored per benign stream, and the same model triggered by our guard leaves 2.35 at 5.3. Spending that budget at random leaves 37.20 frames, so the gain is in the ordering, and a frame detector in the same role gives up almost six, so the judging stage must read the chunk as a clip. The ordering is worth more as the stream lengthens, and the guard carries to a second system. An independent guardrail model agrees with our oracle at $\kappa=0.74$, and a blinded audit by 50 participants puts its precision at 96.1% and recall at 82.8%. Warning. This paper contains examples of generated content that some readers may find disturbing or offensive in nature.

new MODULE: A Mutual-Promoting Deep Unfolding Framework Towards Degradation-Robust Multi-modal Image Fusion

Authors: Yunfei Deng, Han Xu, Jiayi Ma, Guangcan Liu

Abstract: Multi-modal image fusion is crucial for comprehensive scene representation, yet real-world degradations compromise its efficacy, necessitating degradation-robust fusion paradigms. Existing degradation-robust multi-modal image fusion methods are often hindered by cascaded sub-optimal solution or black-box opacity. To address these challenges, we propose a theory-inspired mutual-promoting deep unfolding framework. It reformulates degradation-robust image fusion as a joint optimization problem, utilizing the degradation model and modality generation mechanism to explicitly model both single-task priors and cross-task dependencies. By decomposing the complex multi-task optimization problem into task-related iterative subproblems, the framework establishes a bidirectional reciprocal information flow between restoration and fusion. This process is further unfolded into a multi-stage deep neural network, where each component explicitly corresponds to a specific mathematical operation. In our framework, the fused cross-modal prior actively regularizes the ill-posed restoration, while the purified modality features continuously refine the fusion output. It ensures a transparent architecture that combines the merits of both model-based and data-driven methodologies. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our paradigm achieves state-of-the-art performance while providing superior interpretability. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/Meskend/MODULE.

URLs: https://github.com/Meskend/MODULE.

new GAAFormer: Geometry-Anchored Attention Transformer for Joint Point Cloud Denoising and Upsampling

Authors: Haoxi Tian (School of Civil Engineering,Geomatics, Southwest Petroleum University)

Abstract: Point cloud denoising and upsampling require samples that accurately recover the underlying surface from noisy or sparse observations. A central challenge in both tasks is preserving surface coverage while refining point locations. We introduce GAAFormer (Geometry-Anchored Attention Transformer), a unified generative octree transformer that performs both tasks with a shared network. Its decoder anchors attention neighborhoods to geometry, providing spatial context for occupancy prediction and point generation. Fixed voxel windows and alternating shifts preserve the grouping of surviving nodes as the output tree changes. The design retains the encoder, positional encoding, prediction heads, and parameter count of a serialized-attention backbone. We establish a deletion-consistency property of the neighborhood rule and evaluate both tasks using the same model. On PUNet, GAAFormer achieves a mean Chamfer distance (CD) of $6.0643\times10^{-5}$, reducing CD and Hausdorff distance by 3.35% and 19.34%, respectively, relative to the serialized backbone. For upsampling, it achieves CD values of $0.8475$, $1.7057$, and $1.0147$ ($\times10^{-5}$) on PU-GAN, PU1K, and Sketchfab, respectively, with improvements in CD, Hausdorff distance, and point-to-surface distance over the same backbone on PU-GAN and Sketchfab. These results establish a single architecture for point cloud denoising and upsampling. Within the shared encoder-decoder, the same geometry-anchored attention module improves both noise removal and dense point generation without task-specific redesign or additional parameters.

new SIR-Agent: Skill-Augmented Image Restoration Agent with Recursive Self-Improvement

Authors: Yijian Wang, Qingsen Yan, Weiyu Zhou, Mingde Zhang, Axi Niu, Haokui Zhang, Jiantao Zhou

Abstract: Agents for Image Restoration (IR) aim to leverage Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) to autonomously perceive image degradation and invoke IR tools. However, existing IR agents typically rely on fine-tuning MLLM parameters to adapt to new IR tasks, leading to catastrophic forgetting of IR knowledge. To address this limitation, we propose a skill-augmented IR agent, dubbed SIR-Agent, that maintains IR knowledge in a scalable skill library and uses Recursive Self-Improvement (RSI) to facilitate efficient skill-library evolution. Specifically, we design a Chain Feedback-driven Skill Revision (CFSR) to evolve the skill library. The CFSR analyzes restoration chains to identify and revise weak operations in existing skills. The compatibility of a revised skill with known tasks decides if it replaces the original skill or is appended independently. Furthermore, to advance CFSR's skill-library evolution, we present an RSI-driven skill meta-learning mechanism, which identifies CFSR's inefficient operations and autonomously refines its revision strategy. Extensive experiments across 14 IR benchmarks, covering 6 single-degradation and 8 mixed-degradation scenarios, demonstrate SIR-Agent's superior IR performance. Further evaluation on 7 unseen tasks validates SIR-Agent's generalization to new IR tasks.

new InterBand-EMG: A Cross-Band Foundation Model for Generalizable Hand-Pose Estimation

Authors: Zongheng Guo, Tao Chen, Dalong Du, Yijian Yang, Heng Liu, Lun Shu, Chen Luo, Junhao Tu, Lei Shu, Mingzhou Wu, Yifan Liu, Yu Zhang, Zhenqiang Liu, Shilei Wen, Jie Zhou, Manuela Ferrario

Abstract: Surface electromyography (sEMG) enables continuous hand-pose estimation, but learning representations that generalize across users and movements remains challenging. We introduce InterBand-EMG, which combines band-specific quantization with cross-band reconstruction and structured masked code prediction to learn transferable EMG representations for hand-pose tracking. We also introduce SynapMyo, an eight-channel EMG-to-pose dataset comprising 1,227.74 hand-hours from 499 users across 68 movement categories. Experiments on emg2pose and SynapMyo show that InterBand-EMG outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in joint-angle accuracy across all three generalization settings on both datasets. The source code and the SynapMyo dataset will be publicly released upon acceptance of this paper.

new EgoLAM: Learning Latent Actions from Hand and Head Motion

Authors: Ziyi Ding, Yiwei Wang, Zhongxing Wei, Xianxin Lai, Yechi Liu, Xiaodi Huang, Jinghua Hou, Xiaoquan Sun, Haijier Chen, Zhe Liu, Yinan Chen, Junqiao Zhao, Xiao-Ping Zhang, Jiayu Chen

Abstract: Latent action models let a robot policy learn from video by turning the change between two frames into a training target. In egocentric video, a cup can move across the image because a hand pushes it or because the wearer turns. Both changes help predict the next frame, but only the first describes manipulation. During training, EgoLAM supervises the action code with measured wrist motion and supplies camera motion separately to the forward model. Even when the wearer turns while pushing a cup, the training target still describes how the hand pushed it. The action encoder pools object slots around each hand, and wrist translation and rotation can be decoded from the code in a common reference frame. Extracting a code then requires only two RGB frames. Alongside LARYBench classification and regression, we measure how much wrist motion a frozen code retains and how strongly it responds to a synthetic view or lighting change. EgoLAM has the highest hand-motion decoding score among eight evaluated models. Across three LIBERO suites, policies pretrained on its codes and fine-tuned on expert demonstrations achieve 79.38% mean success, 28.73 points above expert behavior cloning and 9.76 points above the strongest other latent action model in each suite on average.

new A Vision-Based Framework for Manual Assembly Verification and Adaptive Operator Assistance

Authors: Naveen Joseph Delphin, Leonardo Venturoso, Isabella Soraruf, Marco Todescato

Abstract: Manual assembly remains difficult to monitor because operator actions, components, and task progress must be interpreted together. This paper presents a modular framework based on a single overhead red-green-blue (RGB) camera, combining object detection, hand-motion tracking, region-aware interaction detection, finite-state verification, task-specific pose classification, and configurable visual guidance. The system was evaluated during pneumatic-piston assembly and disassembly with eight participants under three assistance conditions: full, mid, and no guidance. Mixed-effects analysis identified an association between assistance condition and a composite measure of motion quality, with lower estimated scores under mid and no guidance relative to full guidance. However, the condition-by-trial interaction was not statistically significant, and the estimated differences in learning trajectories remain descriptive. No condition differences were detected in completion time or failed-stage counts. The study demonstrates the integration of visual perception, procedural verification, and configurable operator feedback in a single-camera workflow. Selecting assistance according to operator expertise remains a design objective for future investigation.

new Can Reasoning and Image Description Enhance Yes/No Visual Mathematical Understanding?

Authors: Guangcheng Song, Weichuan Wang, Linqi Song

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have achieved strong performance on general visual tasks, yet their ability to understand mathematical images remains limited. This paper investigates whether explicit reasoning and textual image descriptions can enhance VLMs' performance on binary yes/no visual mathematical questions. To achieve this, we curated a paired evaluation set to benchmark prominent open-source VLMs under varying prompt and input conditions. In particular, we leverage Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting and use textual descriptions, either replacing or augmenting visual inputs, to isolate and evaluate mathematical comprehension. new BioCL-POS: Biological Completer-aware Learning with Predictive Observation Selection - A Wild Ginseng Study

Authors: Yulu Wang, Tingting Yao, Taolue Liu

Abstract: Growers and traders rely on root morphology to assess wild ginseng, yet breakage can obscure important structural traits. We study point cloud completion to provide geometric references for morphological analysis. Does providing more observed points always improve 3D completion? We test whether selected subsets improve recovery of the original missing region while keeping the pretrained completer fixed. We introduce BioCL-POS, an external selector that predicts how the completer responds to candidate inputs. For each candidate, we predict the displacement and distance from shared spatial locations to the nearest points in its completion. We use these spatial responses to estimate recovery quality and supervise them with measurements from actual completions. At inference, we select an input from the observed geometry and run the completer once. We evaluate four frozen completers on controlled gaps in ginseng and tree data at 5%,10%, and 20% omission, and examine 30% omission on ginseng. At 5--20% omission, we outperform full-input completion and all five alternative selectors on both datasets with all four completers. We achieve lower mean reconstruction error and higher mean F-score and coverage. We thus show that selecting observations can improve geometric recovery without retraining the completer, providing more accurate geometric references for subsequent morphological analysis.

new SAVIOR: Sample-efficient Adaptation of Vision-Language Models for OCR Representation

Authors: Akshata A Bhat, Sharath Naganna, Saiful Haq, Prashant Khatri, Krishna Chaitanya Reddy Tamataam, Neha Arun, Niyati Chhaya, Pushpak Bhattacharyya

Abstract: Optical Character Recognition (OCR) pipelines and vision-language models exhibit high accuracy on standard OCR and document-understanding benchmarks, yet they systematically underperform on document patterns that are operationally critical in financial workflows. These patterns include vertical or rotated text, logo-embedded vendor names, fine-print regulatory clauses, degraded scans, and complex multi-column layouts. While underrepresented in public datasets, they constitute a substantial portion of real-world failure cases. We introduce SAVIOR, a sample-efficient data curation methodology that targets such high-impact failure scenarios to adapt vision-language models for robust financial OCR. We further propose PaIRS, a structure-aware evaluation metric that measures layout fidelity by comparing pairwise spatial relationships between tokens. Fine-tuning Qwen2.5-VL-Instruct with SAVIOR-Train achieves a word recall of 0.93. new Zing-1.0: A Playable World System Built on Controllable Rendering

Authors: Wenbo Zhang, Guoli Zhang, Xuewen Yu, Xueliang Yang, Jiangqiao Yan, Teng Xia, Xinmiao Wu, Zeyang Tian, Haoze Sun, Jinghui Niu, Hongli Ning, Zhiyuan Liu, Xinna Liu, Weimin Liu, Jialin Liu, Tianpeng Li, Kejun Li, Jun Li, Jiawen Li, Yirong He, Haoyuan Guo, Bosheng Gong, Junhao Gao, Xiaoxiao Fu, Xuezhi Chen, Shengdong Chen, Mingyang Chen, Diying Chen, Yinglun Chai, Yiwen Cai

Abstract: We present Zing-1.0, a Playable World System built on Controllable Rendering. An executable engine resolves player actions under the world's rules and produces proxy video from the updated state. A streaming video model synthesizes the player's view from this proxy, and the runtime delivers it to inform subsequent actions. (1) Principle and system. We formulate Controllable Rendering and define the interfaces among world construction, world execution, observation synthesis, and the interaction runtime. To our knowledge, this is the first implementation-independent specification of a Playable World System. (2) AI-native engine. WorldKit provides structured authoring tools and reusable character, camera, and control components for coding agents to construct executable proxy worlds. It supports extensible interactions and precise geometric motion control while separating executable behavior from final visual appearance. (3) Streaming renderer. We adapt MiniMax-H3 for block-causal autoregressive generation and incremental decoding. Proxy video constrains structure and motion, while text and separate scene and subject references specify appearance. We train the generator on diverse tasks and distill it to generate each block in a few denoising steps. The renderer leads the compared streaming editors on OpenVE (3.90) and LongV2VBench (3.44). On StreamEval, Zing-1.0 scores 57.86 on instruction editing and 54.06 on reference editing, exceeding the published baselines on both tracks. (4) Interaction infrastructure. We build an Interaction Runtime that routes player actions to WorldKit while supporting in-play prompt and reference updates. Cross-block joint execution and sparse attention with compressed K/V reduce inference overhead. With the DiT on a single H200 and encoding and decoding on one H100 each, the serving system achieves a steady-state throughput of 23.82 FPS at $832\times480$.

new Unimodal Shortcuts and Joint Scene-Intent Dependence in Vision-Language Safety Representations

Authors: Guijia Zhang, Hongbo Qiu, Guohao Cai, Hao Zheng, Jiajun Zha, Hanqing Wang, Zhenhao Zhang, Huihao Jing, Shing-Chi Cheung, Haoxuan Che, Harry Yang

Abstract: The safety of a request to a vision-language model often depends on the image: asking how to serve a dish is harmless for fresh food and harmful for spoiled food. High accuracy on situational safety benchmarks does not tell us whether a model's safety representation encodes both the scene and the user's intent, because a benchmark can often be solved from one modality alone. Studying frozen vision-language safety representations, we audit five public situational benchmarks and find that each admits a unimodal shortcut: images alone predict the labels, and on same-image prompt pairs a probe that never sees the photograph reaches 0.9645 AUROC. We therefore build a controlled evaluation that crosses safe and hazardous scenes with observation and action requests, so that no single-modality rule can exceed 0.500 group-ranking accuracy. On this control, linear probes on intermediate states of Qwen3-VL-8B and SmolVLM2-2.2B reach 0.958 and 0.930, showing that the scene-intent conjunction is linearly decodable; concatenating separately encoded image and text states already recovers most of this signal, so decodability is not evidence of a fusion mechanism. The same probes screen cheaply but generalize poorly to unseen images and other benchmarks. Reliable multimodal safety evaluation therefore needs controls that rule out unimodal shortcuts before accuracy is read as evidence of joint understanding.

new CoGITAtion: Collaborative Gaze-based Interpretable Task-adaptive Representation for Privacy-sensitive Applications

Authors: Mayar Elfares, Yasmine Elfares, Andreas Bulling

Abstract: Gaze-based analysis provides rich behavioural information for privacy-sensitive applications, but deployment is challenged by heterogeneous, scarce, and distributed data. We present CoGITAtion (Collaborative Gaze-based Interpretable Task-Adaptive Representation), a framework for privacy-preserving inference over vertically distributed gaze data. CoGITAtion transforms variable-length gaze recordings into compact, interpretable gaze-transition graphs and descriptors computed locally by each data owner. Task-adaptive models accommodate heterogeneous and missing feature blocks through task and availability gating, avoiding unnecessary private computation. Results show that shared components capture patterns that generalise across tasks, while task-specific components provide capacity without increasing model size. Public availability gating further reduces encrypted computation by removing branches determined by publicly known task and owner availability without altering predictions. Overall, CoGITAtion enables gaze model reuse, efficient privacy-preserving deployment, and robust inference under incomplete owner availability while retaining interpretable gaze representations.

new ReSA: Preserving Reference-Specified Foreground in In-Context Segmentation

Authors: Dezhao Su, Bingxu Hou, Jingdi Lei, Lin Qian, Likai Wang, Yunyun Wan

Abstract: In-context segmentation uses annotated references to specify the target and its extent in a query image. Accurately transferring this reference-specified foreground requires recovering target regions while suppressing irrelevant predictions. We propose ReSA, a reference-guided framework that unifies query adaptation and mask selection through a shared reference kernel. ReSA leverages foreground and background reference prototypes to constrain prediction changes during adaptation and guide foreground selection through reference-weighted reconstruction. This shared guidance promotes consistent use of reference information across prediction refinement and mask formation. ReSA further selects among adapted predictions and aligns boundary refinement with the selected foreground threshold. We establish a theoretical bound connecting reference-response changes during adaptation and soft reconstruction. With a frozen visual encoder and lightweight test-time adaptation, ReSA requires no offline task training. Ablation studies demonstrate improved foreground retention and effective suppression of false-positive regions, while qualitative results show more complete masks with fewer isolated artifacts. Across ten object, part, and cross-domain segmentation benchmarks, ReSA achieves the highest average mIoU among the evaluated methods, reaching 62.6% in 1-shot and 71.8% in 5-shot.

new PAVE: A Harness for Geometrically Consistent Road Network Modeling with Vision-Language Models

Authors: Yajun Liu, Jinxi Wu, Juan Pablo Mill\'an Raya, Xuesong Zhou

Abstract: Transportation models, whether they simulate traffic or support planning, are only as reliable as the road networks they run on, down to the lanes and turns at each intersection. Practitioners still take such a network from GIS files and supply its junction and movement data by hand, a task that recurs with each new base year and design alternative, or they generate it from open map data. OpenStreetMap's lane tags give the correct layout for only 13 of the 44 approaches we test; open map data are often incomplete or inaccurate. Satellite and street-level imagery, in contrast, cover all 44 and show their lanes as painted. Vision-language models (VLMs) can read such imagery, but asked directly they lose geometric consistency when the same lane must be matched across a satellite crop, street-level photos taken from different poses and an ID-labelled network. We therefore present PAVE (Perceive, Anchor, Verify, Edit), a harness in which code measures what can be measured, traffic engineering knowledge enters as general rules and checks, and the model makes only the open judgements, each anchored to network IDs so that checks catch answers that contradict a measurement, a second reading or a traffic rule. Scored against human labels at signalised intersections, PAVE raises the share of exactly correct lane layouts from 49-64% with a single request to 86-91% for two VLMs, with most of the gain coming from reading one approach at a time on traced lanes. The same division of work edits a real network to a planning brief: the model names the links and nodes of each part of a facility, and code builds the edit from a design template. Of 18 edits, 16 pass every network check, against none when the model writes its own code, and 2 contain a design or coding error, against 7. An analysis shows where VLMs lose geometric consistency and which check catches each failure.

new Borrow the Style, Follow the Prompt: Progressive Reference-Guided Stylization

Authors: Yu Lin, Zi-Jun Wang, Zhilin Zhao

Abstract: Reference-guided text-to-image stylization transfers visual appearance to content specified by text, but the reference subjects and backgrounds can interfere with what the generated image depicts. Suppressing this unwanted information can also discard transferable texture and material cues, creating a tension between content control and faithful preservation of the reference style. Accordingly, we propose ProStyle, which learns a content-suppressed style prior through cross-content supervision on same-style pairs with different subjects and backgrounds and injects it with weak conditioning at auxiliary layers to strengthen style expression. Building on this prior, a complementary pathway learns residual corrections from full reference patch features with the content-suppressed style pathway frozen, before both pathways undergo joint refinement under the same objective. For cross-content learning and evaluation, we introduce ReConStyle, which provides 134K training pairs, two test settings, and object-level measures of target-content fidelity and reference-object overlap alongside style similarity and text alignment. ProStyle outperforms all compared SD1.5-based baselines and achieves the best average rank of 1.86 across four automatic and three human-evaluation criteria on ReConStyle-Full. In the user study, it substantially outperforms the baselines in text alignment and combined suitability, with selection rates of 84.36% and 50.90%, respectively. On the external Style-Rank benchmark, it also achieves the highest CLIP-Text and ImageReward scores.

new VMSA: Video Memory Skill Agent for Long Video Understanding

Authors: Changin Choi, Jungwon Park, Jungmin Ko, Jimyeong Kim, Wonjong Rhee

Abstract: Long-video question answering requires retrieving and integrating relevant evidence from videos spanning hours or even days. Recent agentic approaches organize video information into external memories and iteratively retrieve evidence to answer questions. However, effective retrieval and reasoning remain challenging, as agents must determine what information to search for, which memory sources to access, and how to connect evidence across distant temporal intervals. To address these challenges, we propose Video Memory Skill Agent (VMSA), a framework that jointly designs structured video memories and memory skills for long-video understanding. VMSA constructs three complementary memory components. Segment Caption Memory and Entity Event Memory serve as searchable evidence sources, while Entity-Aware Episode Memory provides broad yet detailed video context to guide evidence retrieval and reasoning. To further support effective memory utilization, VMSA induces memory skills from successful agent trajectories, incrementally refining or extending a compact skill library to guide iterative evidence retrieval, reasoning, and verification. Across seven long-video question-answering benchmarks, VMSA achieves the highest accuracy on all three week-long egocentric benchmarks, outperforming the strongest baseline by up to 9.4% on Ego-R1 Bench, and the best or competitive performance on four additional benchmarks. Ablation studies further demonstrate the contributions of all three memory components and memory skills, with memory skills also improving temporal evidence localization.

new PanoUAV-Track: Full-Spherical UAV Visual Tracking in the Wild

Authors: Bingyu Li, Lichang Yang, Zhiyuan Zhao, Junyu Gao, Xuelong Li

Abstract: Existing UAV tracking benchmarks predominantly rely on perspective cameras with limited fields of view. Omnidirectional cameras enable full-spherical observation, yet tracking from moving UAV platforms remains largely unexplored. Unlike ground-level panoramic tracking, UAV-mounted omnidirectional cameras observe targets across both azimuth and elevation, introducing geometric challenges under equirectangular projection (ERP). To investigate this setting, we capture approximately \textbf{40 hours of real-world 8K panoramic UAV video} and construct \textbf{PanoUAV-Track} through temporal clip extraction, target annotation, and the integration of simulated sequences. The resulting benchmark comprises 1,895 sequences, 507,961 frames, and 2,872 annotated objects, covering real-world daytime and nighttime scenes alongside simulation, and supporting both visual and language-based target specification. Through extensive evaluations, we reveal a clear \textbf{perspective-to-spherical} generalization gap and identify tracking degradation associated with latitude-dependent distortion and ERP seam discontinuity. To address these challenges, we propose \textbf{PanoUAV-Tracker (PUTrack)}, a geometry-aware tracking framework combining latitude-adaptive search and seam-aware periodic sampling. PUTrack improves zero-shot and in-domain tracking, particularly for seam-crossing targets. Code and benchmark resources will be available\footnote{\textcolor{tabpink}{https://libingyu01.github.io/PanoUAV-Track/}}.

URLs: https://libingyu01.github.io/PanoUAV-Track/

new What Stays in the Picture? From Redundant Instructions to Dependency-Aware Image Editing

Authors: Zi-Jun Wang, Yu Lin, Zhilin Zhao

Abstract: We introduce complex redundant-instruction image editing, where a single request specifies multiple objects, their attributes, and spatial relations while revising or canceling earlier editing requirements. Direct editing can fail to recover this intent, while splitting the request into successive steps can preserve obsolete edits and visual errors. Our key insight is to resolve the revision history into a final editing target that jointly guides planning and verification, so that obsolete requests do not become visual edits. Based on this insight, CRIE-Agent learns to consolidate revisions into object-level final intent and executes the retained objects in reference-first blocks, establishing referenced objects before their dependents. Verification then guides complete re-execution from the source image, with conditional layout hints, preventing visual errors from failed attempts from being carried into subsequent ones. We also construct CRIE-S, a benchmark containing 10,000 complex and redundant editing instructions with exact final intent annotations. Across five image-editing backends on CRIE-S, CRIE-Agent raises complete editing success from 0% under direct editing to 27.5--50.0%.

new Beyond What They Say: Cue Acknowledgment and Robustness in Vision-Language Models

Authors: Ali Rahimiakbr, Arvin Baghal Asl, Nima Niroumand, Mohammad Parsa Jafarnejady, Hossein Shahabadi, Amirmohammad Izadi, Mahdieh Soleymani Baghshah

Abstract: Explanations are used to assess whether vision-language models rely on visual evidence or misleading suggestions. A response that explicitly names a cue can still follow it, making disclosure alone an incomplete basis for judging robustness. We ask whether greater cue acknowledgment accompanies greater resistance. We compare matched answers and explanations under helpful, misleading, and conflicting visual and textual cues while holding the underlying question and correct answer fixed. A paired comparison shows that a model can select the endorsed wrong answer more often while acknowledging the cues more often. Reduced silence therefore need not indicate improved resistance. Explanations may also mention a textual suggestion while omitting an accompanying visual cue. Requesting evidence and acknowledgment does not consistently improve resistance in the tested settings. Outcomes vary across models and settings, and these comparisons do not isolate a causal effect of acknowledgment. Evaluations should report cue following and acknowledgment on the same instances, distinguishing visual from textual cues.

new Taking Apart and Putting Together: Tracking Objects Through Physical Transformations

Authors: Davide Gardenal, Mingxuan Liu, Flavio Vella, Quentin Guimard, Elisa Ricci

Abstract: We introduce Transformation Tracking and Understanding (TTU), extending video object tracking to environments where objects undergo physical assemblies and disassemblies. TTU requires continuously tracking objects through transformations, identifying new entities, maintaining a state graph of their relationships, and assigning semantic labels to transformations. While foundation models (e.g., SAM 2/3) maintain tracklets through appearance changes, they cannot update semantic identities as objects physically evolve. Prior work addresses only disassemblies, relying on expensive searches triggered only when tracking fails. We propose Tracking Instances via Proposals and Overlaps (TIPO), a training-free framework that continuously infers transformations via a generate-evaluate-judge pipeline. TIPO generates split and merge proposals from disjoint mask components, scores them against mask-decoder priors using inner and external overlap, and queries an MLLM on the top proposal for visual verification and semantic labeling. To benchmark TTU, we curate Video Instances, States, and Transformation Annotations (VISTA), a dataset including state graphs with 124 assembly and 132 disassembly events across 50 sequences and over 26,000 frames. TIPO achieves state-of-the-art tracking and state-graph accuracy on VISTA, while operating $6\times$ to $15\times$ faster than prior methods. Project page: https://davidegardenal.github.io/tipo-tracking

URLs: https://davidegardenal.github.io/tipo-tracking

new BladeR: Scalable, Shape-Guided 3D Reconstruction of Wind Turbine Blades from UAV Imagery

Authors: Jonathan Sterckx, Michiel Vlaminck, Hiep Luong

Abstract: The structural integrity of wind turbine blades is critical for safe, efficient energy production. Accurate 3D reconstruction allows precise localization and quantification of wear and early damage detection. While drone-based inspections are standard, their high-resolution imagery poses challenges such as weak texture, lighting variability, and high computational cost. We present BladeR, a scalable pipeline for high-quality 3D reconstruction from inspection imagery. A hierarchical sparse reconstruction strategy leverages GPS/IMU data and blade shape priors to guide advanced keypoint matchers on full-resolution images. For dense reconstruction, a tiled Gaussian Splatting framework processes 45 MP images at native resolution, isolates the blade from background, and adaptively enhances detail recovery along the leading edge. new Projector Time Map Calibration for Event-based Structured Light

Authors: Buu-Hoang-Lam Le, Kota Mizutani, Shohei Nobuhara

Abstract: Existing event-based structured light obtains a dense projector-camera correspondence from the timestamp of an event. It assumes that the projector scans a parallel raster and that the sensor timestamps each pixel independently, and we show that neither assumption holds. We obtain the correspondence from a temporal code that identifies the projector pixel directly and therefore needs no prior calibration, we calibrate the projector time map from the same recording, and we recover the geometry of the system and the offsets between its clocks by a spatio-temporal self-calibration in a preamble of a few seconds that needs no reference object and no second camera.

new Pose-based Co-speech Gesture Detection

Authors: Frances Yung, Ninlawat Phuangschoke

Abstract: Co-speech gesture detection identifies the spans in which a speaker gestures (e.g., beat or semantic gestures) in video. As a first step in gesture annotation, it is crucial for studying human communicative behaviour. Despite the availability of automatic tools and models, co-speech gesture detection remains underexplored, and its current state of the art is unclear: existing methods have been evaluated using different datasets, experimental setups, and metrics, making their performance difficult to compare. To address this gap, we benchmark existing detectors using the same cross-validation folds across three datasets (MULTISIMO, GESRes, and CABBS), with evaluation at the frame, gesture-span, and window levels. new AppCon-Bench: Perception and Misperception of Material Appearance in Vision-Language Models

Authors: Santiago Jimenez-Navarro, Ana Serrano, Belen Masia

Abstract: Identifying the same material across changes in illumination or geometry is a critical ability for robust vision systems. Humans exhibit remarkable material appearance constancy, being able to recognize the intrinsic physical properties of an object's surface material across variations in extrinsic, confounding factors such as the lighting or the shape of such object. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) demonstrate strong performance across a wide range of semantic vision tasks, yet their ability to interpret subtle mid-level perceptual attributes remains less understood. We introduce AppCon-Bench, a benchmark comprising five tasks designed to assess material appearance constancy under controlled variations in geometry and illumination, and evaluate representative VLMs alongside human observers, investigating their ability to maintain consistent material judgments across viewing conditions and experimental contexts. new Ego2Ego: Generative Visual Perspective Taking Across Collaborators

Authors: Chuhan Jiao, John Gideon, Anshul Gupta, Guy Rosman, Andreas Bulling

Abstract: Visual perspective taking, the ability to infer what another person sees from their position, is fundamental to human collaboration. However, prior works reduced perspective taking to state estimation, geometric reconstruction, or discrete question answering, whereas existing cross-view video generation methods either inferred the target view from the input video alone or required an external camera that observes the target person. We introduce generative visual perspective taking: Given an ego person's egocentric video, a scene point cloud, and a collaborator's camera trajectory, the task is to generate the continuous egocentric video from the collaborator's perspective. This task is challenging given extreme viewpoint shifts, dynamic occlusions, and sparse visual overlap. We address these challenges with Ego2Ego -- a set of backbone-agnostic geometric modules that provide a pretrained video diffusion model with the 3D scene geometry shared by both interactants. Ego2Ego renders dense, temporally consistent scene coordinate maps into every view as a shared spatial reference, aligns cross-view tokens in 3D ray space through relative geometric attention, and recovers regions outside the ego person's current view from the ego person's visual memory. Experiments on the CoMind dataset across three video diffusion backbones show that Ego2Ego consistently improves visual quality, Level-2 scene layout accuracy, and collaborator motion, outperforming state-of-the-art cross-view video generation baselines on every backbone. By generating the collaborator's egocentric video more accurately, Ego2Ego brings visual perspective taking to human-robot collaboration, where it can help infer what a human collaborator sees and does, and can turn single-person recordings into synchronised multi-perspective videos for robot learning.

new Cecilia: Towards Layout-Inclusive Optical Music Recognition

Authors: Congren Dai, Zhancheng Guo, Xiaobing Li, Maosong Sun

Abstract: Reconstructing an editable music score requires musical events, sung text and the instructions that organise them on the page. Existing recognition targets often omit part of this information, while direct MusicXML prediction repeats substantial structural markup. We introduce Cecilia, a compact representation of canonicalised MusicXML that retains musical content, lyric attachments and layout instructions. Cecilia-OMR predicts this representation through metadata and measure-bounded music segments from shared visual memory. A learned count head determines the number of output segments. Across paired complete scores under a common tokeniser, the ratio of mean MusicXML to Cecilia sequence lengths is 27.73. Cecilia-OMR outperforms Legato 2 on Camera String Quartets and both Lieder conditions, with a four-condition OpenScore macro normalised edit distance of 0.2232 against 0.2998. Separate evaluations of lyrics, notation trees and rendered layout reveal complementary aspects of reconstruction and quantify the contribution of layout instructions. new PathOPEN: A pathologist-curated histopathology dataset for visual question answering

Authors: Awais Naeem, Zhongling Xu, George Huang, Amy Coffey, Nkechi Okonkwo, Mario Saab-Chalhoub, John Lee Sang, Dina Kokh, Muhammad Zulfiqar, Ankita Shukla, Hairong Wang, Chandra Krishnan, Ying Ding

Abstract: PathOPEN is a histopathology dataset for visual question answering. Two clinical pathologists selected 157 cases with 229 available original images and wrote open-ended, five-option multiple-choice and close-ended (yes/no) questions. new Which Dwarf Is This? Doppeldwarf-306 and Near-Identical Instance Retrieval

Authors: Turhan Can Kargin

Abstract: Most established instance-retrieval benchmarks emphasise landmarks; small sculptures from visually similar series offer a complementary challenge. We introduce Doppeldwarf-306: 1,691 openly licensed photographs of 306 bronze dwarf statues in Wroc{\l}aw, Poland, many made in themed series that share one style, with the photographer of each image recorded. With no training, nearest-neighbour search over frozen DINOv2 features names the right statue first for 93.1% of leave-one-out queries, and the best of thirteen backbones from seven pretraining paradigms reaches 94.7%. Accuracy falls slowly as candidates are added: for DINOv2, by about 0.8 points each time their number doubles. A natural design for a phone guide compares a photograph only with statues near the user. In simulation this helps, but less than an equal number of randomly chosen statues does, so random subsampling overstates its value; a permutation test ties the penalty to the real layout (p = 0.005). With the query's photographer withheld, learned local-feature matching raises DINOv2 accuracy from 81.8% to 85.5% and also improves DINOv3. new Solar-Conditioned Flow Matching for Multitemporal Aerial Visible-to-Infrared Image Translation

Authors: Wenwen Fang, Bingwei Hui, Xin Chen, Zhongzhen Sun, Zhenfeng Shao

Abstract: Aerial visible-to-infrared (V2IR) image translation provides scalable thermal data across the full diurnal cycle. However, existing methods predominantly learn a static mapping within narrow acquisition windows. As the thermal state evolves throughout the day, a single visible image may correspond to multiple plausible IR appearances, making V2IR translation an inherently multitemporal task. This letter proposes solar-conditioned latent flow matching (Solar-Flow), which upgrades aerial V2IR translation from static mapping to multitemporal generation. First, we design three computable physical factors, namely the solar elevation, the thermal phase clock (TPC), and the flight altitude. The TPC counts the elapsed heating and cooling hours on a continuous 24-hour axis anchored at sunrise, so the thermal state stays well defined even at hours without training samples. Second, we inject the condition into every Transformer block of a pretrained flow matching generator through adaptive layer normalization. This design preserves translation fidelity and enables multitemporal generation. Finally, we construct a paired aerial V2IR dataset covering 14 hour groups. Solar-Flow achieves multitemporal generation on this dataset, where a single visible image is translated into plausible IR images at multiple times of day, even at hours that were never seen in training.

new Self-Supervised Continuous-Time Depth Learning and Ego-Motion Estimation from Asynchronous Events

Authors: Sadiq O. Macaulay, Dipon Kumar Ghosh, Hadfield Simon

Abstract: Event cameras register brightness changes pixel by pixel with microsecond precision, whereas the dense depth that could supervise them is costly to acquire and exists only at isolated instants. Condensing the stream into frames eases learning, but at the expense of the very timing that sets event sensing apart. We propose a self-supervised approach in which each event is assigned its own depth in continuous time, and the camera motion is recovered from the same observations. Supervision arises from geometry: lifted into 3D by its predicted depth and displaced by the camera motion at the instant it fired, an event should coincide with others produced by the same edge, so any misalignment exposes an error in depth or motion. Differentiable rendering then turns these per-event estimates into dense depth maps at target times within each window, faithful to the events behind them. On MVSEC, the approach outperforms prior event-only self-supervised methods in most sequences and depth ranges, with the widest margins at night, and recovers camera rotation more accurately than an earlier unsupervised event-based method. Ablations isolate the roles of local spatiotemporal context, the temporal backbone, and the alignment objective. These results position motion-compensated event alignment as a principled source of supervision for continuous-time depth and ego-motion estimation.

new PGS-PCQA: Perceptually Gated Synthetic Supervision for No-Reference Point Cloud Quality Assessment

Authors: Qinyuan Fan, Meng Zhang, Clemens G\"uhmann

Abstract: Existing no-reference point cloud quality assessment (NR-PCQA) methods perform well within individual datasets but generalize poorly across datasets. We identify insufficient distortion coverage in training data as a key cause: on unseen distortion types, models rank severity levels near chance or even in reverse. We propose perceptually gated synthetic supervision for no-reference point cloud quality assessment (PGS-PCQA), an NR-PCQA model trained with synthetic distortion ranking. It builds a synthetic distortion bank from source reference point clouds and uses within-type severity ranking as supervision in addition to the source mean opinion scores (MOS), requiring no subjective scores for the synthetic samples. Since higher severity does not always mean lower perceived quality, a perceptual gate softens the ranking targets of pairs with small perceptual differences in rendered views. The synthetic supervision is used only during training and needs no target-domain data. In a single training run on SJTU-PCQA, PGS-PCQA achieves Spearman rank correlation coefficients (SRCCs) of 0.709 on WPC and 0.751 on BASICS. The corresponding published DSP-PCQA scores are 0.595 and 0.723. Under reference-disjoint cross-validation, PGS-PCQA achieves SRCCs of 0.955 on SJTU-PCQA, 0.934 on WPC, 0.906 on BASICS, and 0.791 on LS-PCQA Part I. In the exclusion experiment, the reported scores for the reduced synthetic bank represent 58-65% of the full-bank SRCC gain relative to training without the bank. Code is available at https://github.com/Fan-QY/PGS-PCQA.

URLs: https://github.com/Fan-QY/PGS-PCQA.

new Beyond a Single Human Demonstration: Learning from Scored Trajectory Alternatives for End-to-End Driving

Authors: Xinyao Rao, Zecheng Tang, Liuxuan Sun, Zheyuan Lai, Minjun Yu, Xun Cao, Wei Li

Abstract: Human driving logs record only one trajectory per scene, leaving other feasible driving choices without explicit trajectory targets or quality labels. This limits supervision for end-to-end planners that generate and refine multiple candidate trajectories. Meanwhile, conditioning refinement on a large vision-language model adds substantial online inference cost. We propose DriveSTA, which extends autoregressive proposal generation and proposal-conditioned residual flow refinement with scored trajectory alternatives. For each training scene, we generate alternatives offline through smooth deformations of the recorded trajectory and evaluate their simulated execution against logged traffic. Diverse, high-quality alternatives supervise distinct proposals and define residual targets for quality-guided denoising, while candidates spanning the full score range train a scorer to assess driving quality. A shared joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA), adapted through masked video-feature prediction, encodes observed frames once and provides temporal context for both refinement and scoring. On NAVSIM v1 Navtest, DriveSTA ranks first among the compared planners trained on NAVSIM data alone. With expanded training data, DriveSTA-Scale achieves state-of-the-art results among the compared methods on Navtest and closed-loop HUGSIM, while reducing mean planner forward-pass latency by 73% relative to ChainFlow-VLA in matched evaluations.

new PhiJEPA: Denoising Latent Predictions for Visual Representation Learning

Authors: Makoto Yamada, Yao-Hung Hubert Tsai

Abstract: Can a predictor trained to infer hidden image regions still help a world model plan when part of an object disappears from view and the visible context is blurred? We introduce PhiJEPA, a joint-embedding predictive architecture that adapts PhiNet's two-stage prediction to masked Transformers. From sparse, photometrically corrupted context, a Transformer block predicts hidden tokens of an independently corrupted teacher view. A token-wise MLP then maps these predictions to clean teacher tokens. Controlled ablations show that the combined objective improves dense features over the tested one-stage alternatives. Pre-trained on ImageNet-1K with $5.6\times10^{19}$ training FLOPs, PhiJEPA achieves 69.3 mIoU on linear PASCAL VOC segmentation, exceeding the evaluated I-JEPA and DSeq-JEPA checkpoints in segmentation accuracy, with 25\% fewer training FLOPs than I-JEPA. It also improves hidden-region segmentation over Simple I-JEPA (49.1 vs.\ 44.3 mIoU). As an application, we use the frozen encoder in DINO-WM on PushT, and reuse the pre-training predictor, which JEPAs usually discard, to complete occluded tokens without fine-tuning. In open-loop planning with simultaneous object occlusion and Gaussian blur ($\sigma=2$ pixels), predictor completion raises success from 33\% to 65\%, compared with 25\% for Simple I-JEPA with its predictor and 40\% for iBOT with pixel inpainting. Together, these results support two-stage latent prediction as an efficient approach to learning visual representations that remain useful under photometric degradation and support completion under partial observation.

new EchoAlign: Echocardiography foundation model with multilevel report alignment

Authors: Ahmed Aly, Darya Taratynova, Lotfi Abdelkrim Mecharbat, Roman A. Sandler, Joseph Z. Sokol, Joshua Penn, Klaus H. Maier-Hein, Numan Saeed, Mohammad Yaqub

Abstract: Echocardiography assesses the structure and function of the heart. Cardiologists read each study across several standard views and report the findings visible in those views by cardiac structure. AI foundation models can learn to analyze these studies from clinical reports, reducing annotation burden and supporting clinical interpretation. However, existing models are trained by pairing each video with the full clinical report, including structures and findings its view does not show. This mismatch lets a model associate a finding with incidental or spurious signals rather than its visual evidence, which limits accuracy, anatomical grounding and clinical trust. Here we introduce EchoAlign, a vision-language foundation model developed on 2.87 million echocardiographic videos and images from 159 clinical centers. EchoAlign aligns each cardiac structure, video and study with its report text, and a visibility matrix restricts supervision to the views that actually depict each structure. We compare it with five public foundation models on 0.47 million test videos across ten unseen external datasets from more than 25 clinical centers. EchoAlign achieves the highest mean AUROC of 0.890 (95% CI, 0.881 to 0.897) for classification and the highest mean quadratic weighted kappa of 0.530 (95% CI, 0.511 to 0.544) for severity grading. Across 62 clinical findings, EchoAlign provides one model that detects findings, grades their severity, estimates measurements and segments cardiac structures.

new PaMuk-Bench: A Parallel Multilingual Multitask Benchmark for Vision-Language Tasks

Authors: Silviu-Florin Gheorghe, Radu-Stefan Popa, Adriana Valentina Costache, Adrian-Gabriel Chifu, Alessia Amelio, Paul Irofti, Anders Skaarup Johansen, Lallouani Bouchakour, Mohamed Lichouri, Khaled Lounnas, Neelu Madan, Yashvir Malik, Radu Tudor Ionescu

Abstract: While vision-language models (VLMs) currently obtain notable results for highly-studied languages, e.g. English, they exhibit significant performance drops in certain low-resource languages, especially due to the lack of annotated datasets. To this end, we propose PaMuk-Bench, a novel parallel multilingual multitask benchmark for vision-language tasks. Our benchmark includes a set of over 10K curated English prompts that are translated into six languages: Arabic, Danish, French, Hindi, Italian, Romanian. Parallel translations, which are obtained via a semi-automatic process, along with accompanying generated and retrieved images enable the comparative development and evaluation of VLMs across a wide range of tasks: text-to-image generation, image captioning, content-based image retrieval, image generation difficulty estimation, image retrieval difficulty estimation. We empirically show that zero-shot English and multilingual pre-trained VLMs compare consistently worse than their fine-tuned counterparts, confirming that PaMuk-Bench is a valuable resource that can drastically reduce the language gap across a variety of VLM architectures and tasks. PaMuk-Bench is available at https://github.com/ghesil/pamuk_bench.
