cross Fooling the Mind Twice: Human Detection of Audiovisual Deepfakes Under Repeated Exposure and Corrective Feedback

Authors: Ammarah Hashmi, Sahibzada Adil Shahzad, Yu Tsao, Hsin-Min Wang

Abstract: Human deepfake detection remains unreliable, and whether reassessment with corrective feedback improves subsequent judgments remains unclear. This study examined differences between initial and repeated deepfake assessments among 110 participants using audiovisual stimuli from the FakeAVCeleb and Deepfake Detection Challenge (DFDC) datasets. Participants evaluated 40 videos from each dataset in phase 1 (PhaseI) and reassessed the same videos in phase 2 (PhaseII) under a procedure combining repeated presentation and corrective feedback. For the DFDC dataset, the participants' mean accuracy increased by 8.64 percentage points from 59.52% in PhaseI to 68.16% in PhaseII (t(109)=7.37, p<.001, d_z=0.70); the logistic model yielded: beta=0.375, SE=0.065, z=5.78, p<.001, {OR}=1.46. cross A Plan or an Adversarial Example? A Round-Trip Check for Latent World Models

Authors: Huiyuan Tian, Chih-Yung Wen, Shijian Li

Abstract: If we optimize an image until a classifier is confident it shows a cat, we may get an adversarial example, not a cat. Planning with a latent world model is a similar search for actions whose predicted future is closest to the goal. Is the result a plan, or an adversarial example of the world model? We show that it can be either. On the released DINO-WM and LeWM, more iterations of the cross-entropy method (CEM) lower the predicted cost but lose up to half of the problems CEM had already solved. The model's own predictions do not reveal these failures. We propose a simple test that executes the plan after every iteration and counts the problems a planner solves and then loses. Without replanning, success rises from 0.39 to 0.76 on Wall with DINO-WM and from 0.56 to 0.94 for MPPI on JEPA-WM. With replanning (MPC), it rises from 0.70 to 0.81 on Cube with LeWM.

cross FM4SAR-Bench 1.0: Towards A Fair Benchmark for Evaluating Foundation Models for SAR Image Interpretation

Authors: Mingyao Chen, Yongxiang Liu, Tianpeng Liu, Yuchen Yong, Jie Zhou, Zhenhao Yang, Pinyi Huang, Weijie Li, Jiehua Zhang, Bowen Peng, Zhimao Peng, Zhiyuan Zhang, Li Liu

Abstract: Foundation models have advanced SAR image interpretation but created a heterogeneous, fragmented model ecosystem. Limited SAR data accessibility also means high reported performance does not guarantee access to training data, code, or weights, limiting reproduction, benchmarking, and deployment. cross Inject, Encode, Navigate: Feature-Wise Time Injection for Rectified Flow with Bidirectional Feature Encoding

Authors: Xiaogang Wang, Debasish Mishra, Jia Hao Toh, Wangsheng Liu, Yash Shah

Abstract: Vision-model-based robotic navigation has gained increasing popularity, yet existing approaches still struggle to deliver effective and efficient navigation performance, \textit{e.g.}, diffusion-based policies that require costly iterative denoising. In this work, we propose three improvements to bridge these gaps. First, we adopt a rectified flow framework to reduce the required inference steps to one while achieving comparable or even superior performance. Second, we leverage a bidirectional Mamba-based visual encoder that employs selective state-space modeling to more effectively capture bidirectional temporal dependencies across observation frames, even with longer input sequences. Most importantly, we find that na\"{i}vely combining these two components to balance accuracy with inference speed yields no further performance gains or even produces inferior results. Upon extensive analysis, we realized that the root cause lies in the fact that the Hydra-derived temporal context is shared unchanged across all flow timesteps, and timestep information is introduced only within the velocity network. This weak coupling limits the model's ability to adaptively exploit temporal visual features at different flow states. To address this, we propose a feature-wise timestep conditioning design that explicitly conditions the visual embedding on the flow timestep, enabling noise-level-aware trajectory generation, thereby restoring a meaningful gradient pathway and enabling coherent interaction between the rectified flow and the temporal encoder. Extensive quantitative and qualitative experiments across multiple datasets demonstrate that our approach consistently outperforms both baseline and competitive methods.

cross Tripwire: Robot Agents that Program Their Own Interrupts under Human Disturbance

Authors: Sunli Chen, Yun Zong, Qinhong Zhou, Xiaowen Qiu, Jiaben Chen, Zeyuan Yang, Chuang Gan

Abstract: Agent harnesses are turn-based: the agent acts, waits for the result, then reasons. When the action invokes a pretrained robot policy, such as a vision-language-action (VLA) or world action model (WAM), the policy may react continuously to observations, but the agent cannot revisit its decision until the call returns. Meanwhile, a person may step into the robot's path or move the object it is about to grasp. Invoking the agent more often narrows this blind interval, but a reasoning model takes seconds per decision and most control steps need no new one. We propose TRIP, a training-free, event-driven harness in which the agent programs its own interrupts. With each call, the agent writes a tripwire: a lightweight, stateful Python program that runs alongside the policy, can query a compact VLM (for example, to locate a person and measure their distance), and decides whether the call continues or control returns to the agent. When it fires, the harness halts the policy and returns control with the reason; the agent then reassesses, adapts, and continues under a rewritten tripwire. We evaluate TRIP with three VLAs and one WAM on RoboHD, a benchmark of 168 RoboCasa365 scenes in which a simulated person disturbs the robot and any human-robot contact counts as failure. Compared with a turn-based harness using the same agent and tools, TRIP halves the rate of human contact with the strongest policy, raising safety from 61.3% to 81.3%, without sacrificing task success (38.7% vs. 31.0%). Real-robot experiments show that TRIP completes two manipulation tasks under human interference where the turn-based harness and the policy alone largely fail.

Abstract: Task-oriented image signal processors (ISPs) adapt RAW-to-RGB processing for downstream perception. cross Preserving Pretrained CFG Structure in RL for Diffusion Models with Conditional Contrast Regularization

Authors: Yunfei Zheng, Xiaolong Wang, Dandan Zheng, Xianjun Deng, Shenghao Liu, Wei Xiang, Yuqian Li, Laurence Tianruo Yang

Abstract: Reinforcement Learning (RL) has become an important component in the post-training of diffusion models. In practice, however, standard RL often improves the target reward without reliably preserving holistic generation quality, leading to unstable reward-quality trade-offs that we view as a form of reward hacking. Since Classifier-Free Guidance (CFG) is known to improve visual clarity, prompt alignment, and perceptual quality in generation, we investigate whether its underlying structure can serve as a useful prior during RL. We present a unified geometric view that interprets both CFG and RL as correction vectors in a common prediction space, and identify the projection of the RL-induced correction onto the pretrained CFG correction as a useful diagnostic of quality-related structural preservation. Geometric analysis and rotation intervention further show that the pretrained CFG direction carries information useful for generation quality that reward-driven RL updates do not necessarily preserve. Based on this insight, we propose Conditional Contrast Regularization (CCR), which uses the frozen reference model's conditional contrast as a local structural prior during RL. Experiments across multiple reward settings and backbones show that CCR more consistently preserves generation quality while maintaining competitive reward, yielding a better reward-quality trade-off than standard RL and generic regularization methods.

Authors: Yongkang He, Xiaofen Xing, Xiangmin Xu

Abstract: Action-conditioned video world models imagine future observations under candidate robot actions, providing a promising visual tool for robot policy evaluation and interaction simulation. cross HR-Merge: Model Merging with Headroom-Weighted Residual Refinement

Authors: Jiakang Huang, Xueyan Zhang, Renyi Cai, Jingcheng Niu, Jiaru Li, Yining Wang, Josh Zhang, Jiahe Jiang, Jinman Zhao, Gerald Penn, Baharan Mirzasoleiman

Abstract: Model merging combines experts fine-tuned from a shared backbone, such as LoRA adapters of a large language model, into one multi-task model without further training. Existing methods apply one rule to every task, yet the accuracy they lose is uneven across tasks. We show that the lost accuracy remains recoverable from each task's residual, its task vector minus the merged update, and that a task's validation headroom, the share of its expert validation accuracy the merge loses, indicates where recovery pays off. Residuals from different tasks interfere, so they must be merged rather than summed. We propose HR-Merge, a training-free wrapper around any merging method. It weights each residual by its task's headroom, re-merges the weighted residuals with the base operator, and adds the result back at a strength selected on validation, falling back to the base merge when no strength helps. On six NLI adapters of LLaMA-3 8B, HR-Merge lifts TIES from 93.29 to 94.29 normalized accuracy, above all tested bases, mainly by recovering its weakest task. In vision, on three CLIP backbones, up to 20 tasks, and LoRA merging, it generally improves or preserves base accuracy and yields the best model in every setting.

cross TrojanMaxxing: One-shotting VLM Backdoors with Weight-Space Programming

Authors: Ahaan Dabholkar, Harshvardhan Patel, Michalis Polychronakis, Saurabh Bagchi

Abstract: Recent work shows that large language models (LLMs) can reason directly about the weights of other neural networks. Given only a natural-language objective, an LLM can inspect a model's weights, understand what they compute, and edit them directly without gradient-based optimization. This capability, termed weight-space programming (WSP), has been used to improve model performance on math reasoning, medical QA and other benchmarks. We propose TrojanMaxxing, a novel strategy to backdoor vision-language models (VLMs), by reframing it as a WSP task. We use Claude Opus 5.5 and Mythos 5.2 agents to backdoor VLMs without any optimization or training methods. The agents inspect the model weights, apply targeted edits, and co-design an imperceptible visual trigger that activates the backdoor. We then study the agents' strategies and present a thorough evaluation of the trojaned models on various benchmark datasets. TrojanMaxxing achieves attack success rates of 93%-100% on content moderation and 78%-100% on OCR while maintaining clean accuracy. Perhaps most consequentially, we show that injecting backdoors in a training-free manner is possible by simply, "asking an agent to do it".

cross Neurodegenerative Differential Diagnosis with Knowledge-enhanced Structural Reasoning

Authors: Mingxuan Wang, Siyuan Du, Yanfeng Wang, Ya Zhang, Chencheng Zhang, Jiangchao Yao

Abstract: Accurate differential diagnosis of neurodegenerative disorders remains challenging due to overlapping clinical manifestations and heterogeneous, often incomplete multimodal observations. Existing multimodal models can integrate imaging and clinical measurements but often overlook disease-specific evidence patterns and pairwise relations among competing diagnoses. We therefore propose Knowledge-enhanced Structural Reasoning (KnowSR), which aligns multimodal patient representations with a literature-grounded knowledge bank of disease prototypes, signed evidence directions, and pairwise diagnostic relations. Specifically, in KnowSR, patient-specific knowledge alignment and target-aware pooling inject structured disease evidence, while anchored relational reasoning reconciles pairwise diagnostic margins over a sparse differential graph, thereby collectively enhancing differential diagnostic capability. cross Mechanism Parity for Simulation-Based Validation of Autonomous-Driving Perception

Authors: Danial Safaei, Siddartha Khastgir, Matthew D. Higgins, Jeroen Ploeg, Mohsen Alirezaei, Son Tong, Paul A. Jennings, Xingyu Zhao

Abstract: Simulation is central to intelligent-vehicle validation, but visual realism or output agreement between a real scene and its synthetic counterpart does not prove that a perception system used the same evidence in both domains. This paper addresses that mechanism-parity gap for paired real-synthetic autonomous-driving perception. It unifies Decisive-Feature Fidelity, which compares decisive evidence maps in a frozen system under test (SUT), with Concept-Decisive Feature Fidelity, which projects decisive evidence into semantic scene concepts for diagnosis. The framework is a validation diagnostic rather than a standalone safety certificate: it asks whether output-consistent simulation also preserves the mechanism by which the perception model reaches that output. The empirical evidence comprises ten retrained diffusion ControlNet generators evaluated on a deterministic exact-geometry KITTI-Virtual KITTI 2 subset. Generated counterparts use a 1248-by-376-pixel padded inference canvas and are cropped back to the exact KITTI dimensions before evaluation. The final benchmark reports target-image, output-consistency, and mechanism metrics recomputed from a single audited output set, then adds metric-disagreement cases, bootstrap uncertainty, threshold sensitivity, and concept/cache integrity audits. Across the benchmark, output and appearance fidelity are at most weakly and inconsistently rank-correlated with decisive-mechanism distance for all three frozen SUTs, and a constructed positive control shows the estimator detects a known planted mechanism that output agreement misses, supporting decisive-evidence diagnostics for intelligent-vehicle simulation validation. All numerical claims are restricted to the declared exact-geometry benchmark, ten generators, and three frozen SUTs.

Authors: Caoyuan Ma, Wendi Liu, Zhimin Zhang, Muyao Niu, Yujie Lu, Zengxi Zhang, Ji Zhao, Shuai Dong, Wenpu Liu, Ziyue Wang, Wenzheng Chang, Taiqiang Wu, Weichu Xie, Yuqi Xu, Haiyuan Wan, Xuehui Wang, Jiaqi Wang, Wenqi Shao, Yinqiang Zheng

Abstract: Computer-use agents now solve most desktop tasks through direct software input, but a robot using the same computer must turn GUI knowledge into physical contact with a keyboard and mouse. The evaluated model receives observations only from the workstation camera views and operates the peripherals through either a single-arm rigid probe or dual-arm dexterous hands. Across five frontier models and 40 tasks in ten applications, models that complete 31-39 tasks on the desktop complete at most 23 with the probe and 28 with the hands. ROSWorld makes the gap between knowing what to do on a computer and physically doing it measurable.

cross From Tokens to Pixels: Training-Free Guided Upsampling of Frozen CARL Features for Hyperspectral Imaging

Authors: Dominik Fromme, Ellen Markowski, Tim Streckert, J\"org Thiem

Abstract: Camera-agnostic foundation models such as CARL map spectral images from arbitrary sensors into a shared feature space, but deliver their features only on a token grid at one eighth of the input resolution. The usual bilinear interpolation blurs material boundaries over about one token and rounds off corners. We investigate how much spatial accuracy image-guided upsampling recovers for frozen CARL features without training the encoder or the upsampler. We compare joint bilateral upsampling (JBU), a guided filter and the pretrained AnyUp, each with the hyperspectral image or an RGB rendering as the guide. A hyperspectral ColorChecker dataset acquired specifically for this study, with model-independent reference geometry measured to sub-pixel precision, serves as the evaluation benchmark. JBU with the hyperspectral guide raises the Boundary IoU from 13 to 67 % and reduces the effective edge width of the features from 38 to 4 px, close to the resolution of the raw spectra. The effect occurs in all 24 patches of the ColorChecker and replicates on an additional recording. On placenta and oral cavity images, the feature edges also become markedly qualitatively sharper. For sharp, high-contrast boundaries, JBU is the most accurate and most economical method. On tissue with softer transitions, the pretrained AnyUp yields qualitatively sharper contours. An analysis of 78 simulated band configurations shows that the gain persists in every configuration as long as the head matches the configuration. Image-guided upsampling is thus a simple, training-free step for applications that use CARL frozen with linear heads.

cross Seeing Is Not Steering: Where Verifiable Reasoning Can Enter an End-to-End Driving Planner

Authors: Gautam Kumar Jain, Atharva Mahindrakar, Shubham Phoolari, Ignacio Alvarez, Carsten Markgraf, Constanze Hungar, Christoph Lauer, Julian Staehler

Abstract: Driving models that reason may describe a hazard correctly while choosing their path for other reasons. We study where scene reasoning can enter an end-to-end planner so its influence can be checked. We freeze Drive-JEPA and use a fine-tuned vision-language model to state scene facts. Although fine-tuning improves fact accuracy, the two learned components we test show no detectable benefit from reading them. A rule-based fact check instead rejects or shortens paths that contradict the facts, linking each intervention to a stated fact. On the frozen planner alone, it gains 2.4 EPDMS points on navhard. Its 2.0-point margin over a control that changes the same frames without consulting facts remains statistically uncertain. A diagnostic using exact facts from maps and annotations yields a 3.6-point margin over its corresponding control, with a 95% confidence interval above zero. Separately, a path scorer and plan memory that read no stated facts raise navhard from 41.2 to 51.8. Bounding travel distance using the reasoner's facts adds 0.3 there and reduces navtest by 2.0. The fact check makes each intervention auditable, while the driving value of the reasoner's facts remains unresolved.

cross SOAR: Training-Free Rank-Consistent Rollout Compression for Latent World Model Planning

Authors: Anqi Li, Kai Liu, Linghe Kong, Yulun Zhang

Abstract: Latent world models plan by rolling out hundreds of candidate action sequences through a transformer predictor, so a single decision can take tens of seconds. Existing accelerations prune spatial tokens as in vision-language models, yet a sampling-based planner uses predicted costs only to rank candidates, so a rollout can be compressed freely as long as the ranking survives. We propose SOAR, a training-free method that adapts rollout compression to each planning decision. At every decision, SOAR probes a few candidates under several token budgets and keeps the smallest budget that preserves their ranking, so it compresses as far as the current decision allows, without task labels. A closed-loop study of more than 80,000 episodes fixes the rest of the rollout: the first step, which alone reads a real observation, stays exact, and the context frame is cached rather than recomputed. On seven navigation and manipulation tasks with frozen checkpoints, SOAR matches dense-level planning success at 2.3x speedup and retains 98% at 2.5x. Our code is available at https://github.com/starrywiki/SOAR.

URLs: https://github.com/starrywiki/SOAR.

cross Beyond Resolution: Inference-Time Parameterized Temporal Consistency in Generative Regional Climate Downscaling

Authors: Luca Glawion, Julius Polz, Harald Kunstmann, Benjamin Fersch, Patrick Ludwig, Antonio S\'anchez-Ben\'itez, Christian Chwala

Abstract: GCM-to-RCM downscaling emulation is dominated by generative diffusion models, but most existing work evaluates skill only per timestep, treating each field as an independent snapshot. This leaves open whether generated sequences are temporally coherent or merely independently plausible, a gap that matters increasingly for sub-daily resolutions since downstream impacts depend on accumulation and persistence, not just on isolated states. This study asks whether autoregressive conditioning can deliver coherent rollouts without error accumulation and ensemble-spread collapse, and whether a single model can expose the resulting coherence-diversity trade-off as a controllable inference choice rather than a fixed design commitment. We introduce a noise-conditioned teacher-forcing training scheme conditioning a diffusion U-Net downscaling model on a noised previous high-resolution state, so the trained model interpolates continuously between autoregressive (AR) and per-timestep independent (PTI) sampling via one noise parameter. We apply the scheme to an hourly, European-domain historical simulation downscaling AWI-CM1 (1$^\circ$) with ICON (0.11$^\circ$, regridded to 0.25$^\circ$) for precipitation and 2-meter temperature. The noise parameter traces a smooth interpolation between ICON's autocorrelation and PTI generation. AR rollouts of up to six months show no progressive degradation, with ensemble spread contracting only slightly within the first 24 hours. In contrast, the same architecture trained on noise-free previous states diverges. For precipitation PTI underestimates maxima by up to 2x in daily aggregations, while AR tracks ICON's maxima closely. For temperature, differences between modes are negligible. These results show that in diffusion downscaling temporal consistency can be achieved architecture-agnostically and offered as a controllable, not fixed, inference choice.

cross Beyond Sample Hardness: Adaptive Data Selection through Learning Dynamics

Authors: Hai Gan, Suorong Yang, Guang Li, Baile Xu, Furao Shen

Abstract: Data selection has emerged as an effective approach to reducing the model training costs. However, most existing methods often rely heavily on sample hardness, which may overlook differences in sample learning dynamics and lead to suboptimal sample selection. In this paper, we propose LeDAS, an end-to-end Learning Dynamics-Aware Selection framework for dynamic data selection, which jointly characterizes sample importance through hardness, learning progress, and deterioration, enabling adaptive sample prioritization throughout training with marginal additional computational overhead. Specifically, we track per-sample loss dynamics using exponential moving averages (EMA) to obtain three complementary signals that capture current learning difficulty, ongoing learning improvements, and potential degradation. To accommodate evolving training dynamics, we introduce Adaptive Metric Fusion, a contextual bandit-based mechanism that dynamically adjusts the relative importance of these signals based on training feedback. Furthermore, a bounded staleness compensation mechanism accounts for historical selection patterns by increasing the selection priority of samples that have not been selected for extended periods. Extensive experiments across diverse vision and language tasks demonstrate the effectiveness and generality of LeDAS. Compared with state-of-the-art baselines, LeDAS consistently achieves superior performance, matching or surpassing full-dataset training at a 50\% selection ratio on ImageNet-1K and in LLM supervised fine-tuning. Moreover, its modular and task-agnostic design enables the integration of complementary selection signals, demonstrating strong robustness to noisy and corrupted datasets.

Authors: Zhenxuan Fan, Zhuoya Wang, Yuqian Yuan, Wenqiao Zhang, Juncheng Li, Jun Xiao, Siliang Tang, Yueting Zhuang

Abstract: Vision-language model (VLM)-based agents offer a promising approach to robotic manipulation, yet remain limited by inadequate memory organization, uncertain failure attribution, and limited reuse of execution experience. cross When Are Images Needed in Multimodal Machine Translation? Text-Only Routing for On-Demand Visual Context

Authors: Xinyu Wang, Yingfeng Luo, Yuxin Gao, Anxiang Ma, Jingbo Zhu, Tong Xiao

Abstract: Image-guided multimodal machine translation (MMT) leverages visual context to resolve translation-relevant uncertainty. However, prevailing systems either process images unconditionally or apply gating only after visual encoding, leaving redundant visual computation unavoidable. To mitigate this computational overhead, routing decisions must rely on a pre-visual source signal. Crucially, surface-level ambiguity does not equate to visual dependence, as intra-sentence textual context often suffices for disambiguation. We therefore formulate visual invocation as source-text sufficiency prediction: determining exclusively from the source text whether translation-relevant uncertainty remains unresolved. To support this formulation, we construct a visual-dependency dataset with three fine-grained labels (no_ambiguity, text_resolved, and vision_needed) and verify discriminative visual evidence for vision_needed instances. We further propose PreVIR, a lightweight, backend-agnostic text-only router that selects between text-only and multimodal branches before computing visual features. Experiments on English-to-Chinese MMT demonstrate that PreVIR achieves a favorable COMET-budget trade-off relative to strong routing baselines. At its default operating point, it substantially reduces visual calls while maintaining translation quality comparable to always-on multimodal inference. Label-wise and case analyses show that this trade-off stems from distinguishing genuine visual dependency from contextually resolved ambiguity.

Abstract: Ultrasound time-harmonic elastography (THE) is a non-invasive imaging technique that measures soft-tissue stiffness. Abstract: Underwater imaging suffers from wavelength-dependent absorption and scattering that distort scenes and obscure spectral signatures. We introduce a physically grounded framework that lifts underwater restoration to continuous spectral radiance using multi-view hyperspectral imaging. Authors: Fabio Guglietta, Tianyi Li, Francesco Viola, Luca Biferale

Abstract: Cardiovascular imaging offers several ways to measure blood flow, but each provides an incomplete, resolution-limited, or noisy view of the underlying dynamics. Here, we investigate generative diffusion models as tools for cardiovascular-flow generation and reconstruction using a fully controlled state-of-the-art dataset from a digital twin of a healthy human left heart. Abstract: The ability to simulate the world in a spatially consistent manner is a crucial requirement for effective world models. replace SVarM: Linear Support Varifold Machines for Classification and Regression on Geometric Data

Authors: Emmanuel Hartman, Nicolas Charon

Abstract: Despite progress in the rapidly developing field of geometric deep learning, performing statistical analysis on geometric data--where each observation is a shape such as a curve, graph, or surface--remains challenging due to the non-Euclidean nature of shape spaces, which are defined as equivalence classes under invariance groups. Building machine learning frameworks that incorporate such invariances, notably to shape parametrization, is often crucial to ensure generalizability of the trained models to new observations. This work proposes \textit{SVarM} to exploit varifold representations of shapes as measures and their duality with test functions $h:\mathbb{R}^n \times S^{n-1} \rightarrow \mathbb{R}$. This method provides a general framework akin to linear support vector machines but operating instead over the infinite-dimensional space of varifolds. We develop classification and regression models on shape datasets by introducing a neural network-based representation of the trainable test function $h$. This approach demonstrates strong performance and robustness across various shape graph and surface datasets, achieving results comparable to state-of-the-art methods while significantly reducing the number of trainable parameters.

replace Reconstruction Alignment Improves Unified Multimodal Models

Authors: Ji Xie, Trevor Darrell, Luke Zettlemoyer, XuDong Wang

Abstract: We discover a simple but effective way for visual understanding to help visual generation in a unified model. Unified multimodal models (UMMs) unify visual understanding and generation within a single architecture. However, conventional training relies on image-text pairs (or sequences) whose captions are typically sparse and miss fine-grained visual details, even when they use hundreds of words to describe a simple image. We introduce Reconstruction Alignment (RecA), a resource-efficient post-training method that leverages visual understanding encoder embeddings as dense "text prompts," providing rich supervision without captions. Despite its simplicity, RecA is broadly applicable: across autoregressive, masked-autoregressive, and diffusion-based UMMs, it consistently improves generation and editing fidelity. replace Verify Before You Generate: Sketch-Guided Efficient Motion-Planning for Video Generation

Authors: Yidong Huang, Zun Wang, Han Lin, Dong-Ki Kim, Shayegan Omidshafiei, Jaehong Yoon, Yue Zhang, Mohit Bansal

Abstract: Recent video generation approaches increasingly rely on planning intermediate control signals like object trajectories to improve temporal coherence and motion fidelity. However, these methods mostly employ single-shot plans that are typically limited to simple motion instructions, leading to suboptimal motion quality, or iterative refinement which requires multiple calls to the video generator and incurs high computational cost. To address this trade-off, we propose SketchVerify, a training-free framework whose key insight is to verify motion plans using lightweight video sketches before invoking the expensive video generator. This lightweight planning space enables efficient comparison of multiple candidate trajectories and bounded iterative refinement before a single final video-generation pass. Given a prompt and a reference image, SketchVerify samples multiple candidate trajectories and renders each as a lightweight video sketch by compositing the moving objects over a static background. A vision-language verifier then ranks these sketches according to instruction alignment and visual motion consistency, allowing unsuitable trajectories to be rejected before full video synthesis. The motion plan is iteratively refined until a satisfactory one is identified, then passed to a trajectory-conditioned generator for final synthesis. Experiments on WORLDMODELBENCH and PHYWORLDBENCH demonstrate consistent improvements in instruction following and temporally coherent, visually plausible motion. Using the same ATI-14B generator, SketchVerify improves the aggregate WorldModelBench score from 7.71 to 8.71. Compared with full-video iterative refinement, it achieves a 13.1x planning-stage speedup and a 3.6x end-to-end speedup.

replace S2WMamba: Modality-Specific Wavelet Decomposition with Learnable Detail Bands for Pansharpening

Authors: Haoyu Zhang, Junhan Luo, Yugang Cao, Liang-Jian Deng

Abstract: Pansharpening reconstructs a high-resolution multispectral image from a high-resolution panchromatic (PAN) image and a low-resolution multispectral (MS) image. The MS bands carry the colour that must be preserved, but they enter most networks as an undifferentiated feature dimension, mixed with the spatial correction before any spectral decomposition is formed. We propose S2WMamba (spatial, spectral, wavelet, Mamba), which treats the spatial and the spectral axes with different transforms: a two-dimensional wavelet decomposition applied to a learned embedding of the PAN image isolates spatial edges and textures, while a one-dimensional wavelet decomposition applied along the spectral axis of the fused cue separates the spectral base shared by all bands from the band-specific variation. In both transforms the approximation (low-pass) filter is fixed and carries no learned parameters, and only the detail filters are learned. The two branches are coupled in one direction: the spatial branch first reconstructs a correction to the upsampled MS image, which then conditions the input of the spectral branch. A Mamba-based aggregator merges the two branch outputs. On the PanCollection benchmark, S2WMamba attains the best reduced-resolution PSNR on each of WorldView-3 (39.502 dB), GF2 (50.083 dB) and QuickBird (38.802 dB), and in full resolution its HQNR is within 0.011 of the best on every sensor. Ablations show that the benefit of the cascade lies entirely in its direction: reversing it yields the same accuracy as removing the coupling altogether. Code: github.com/KagUYa66/S2WMamba; benchmark: github.com/liangjiandeng/PanCollection.
