replace FIRE3D: Feed-forward Interactive 3D Scene Reconstruction Within A Minute

Authors: Hongchi Xia, Tianhang Cheng, Wei-Chiu Ma, Shenlong Wang

Abstract: We present FIRE3D, a unified framework that transforms a single RGB image or casual video into interactable 3D scene assets for games and interactive applications in under a minute for up to twelve textured instances including preprocessing. At the core of FIRE3D is a feed-forward inference pipeline that predicts a compositional scene representation from posed RGB-D observations estimated from the RGB capture, including the 6-DoF pose, bounding box, mesh, and texture for detected objects. By modeling the scene as a collection of discrete entities, FIRE3D produces amodal object assets that can be independently edited and used in interactive applications. Our framework requires no test-time optimization, runs substantially faster than the compared reconstruction systems, and provides object-level completeness beyond existing feed-forward 3D approaches. We demonstrate leading detection accuracy, strong geometry reconstruction, and competitive rendering quality on the evaluated benchmarks, with substantial runtime gains.

replace TailorGS: Made-to-Measure Density Control for 3D Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Afif Boudaoud, Jiayi Liu, Alexandru Calotoiu, Torsten Hoefler

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting reconstructs a scene from a set of posed photographs called the capture; Gaussian count affects reconstruction quality, storage, and rendering cost. How to select this number automatically across capture scales remains unresolved: density control techniques effective on standard benchmarks can leave larger captures with too few Gaussians to reconstruct fine details. We observe that the surface to represent, given by capture extent and resolution, is known before training, whereas the capacity needed to reconstruct its content depends strongly on the 3D optimization trajectory and becomes apparent during reconstruction. We introduce TailorGS, which unifies coverage-scaled exploration and coverage-weighted self-distillation under a non-redundant 3D scene coverage measure. Before training, TailorGS groups observed surface points into coarse voxels and shares each voxel's weight among views observing it from the same side. Combined with image resolution, these weights set a generous exploration cap that limits budget inflation from repeated observations. During the first 80% of training, TailorGS grows toward this cap. The resulting model serves as teacher for a single closed-form self-distillation step, balancing fidelity to its renderings against Gaussian count under the same coverage measure. The retained primitives are refined over the remaining 20% of training. On 13 standard scenes, TailorGS matches the mean PSNR of the best-performing evaluated baseline, LeGS, with 48% fewer Gaussians. On eight large captures, the same configuration automatically scales to larger models when necessary, achieving the highest mean PSNR across 13 evaluated baselines: 0.63 dB above the runner-up by allocating 2.8 times as many Gaussians.

replace MemLife: Curating and Reasoning over Long-Term Egocentric Video Memories

Authors: Guangzhi Xiong, Xinyuan Zhang, Xiao Yang, Hyokun Yun, Kai Zhang, Shiun-Zu Kuo, Hyeonjeong Ha, Xilun Chen, Kai Sun, Lucas Liang, Guangqiang Dong, Ejaz Ahmed, Ahmed A Aly, Anuj Kumar, Raffay Hamid, Aidong Zhang, Xin Luna Dong

Abstract: Long-term egocentric video enables personalized AI assistants to reason about daily life. However, as video histories grow to hundreds of hours spanning months or years, reprocessing raw clips for every query becomes computationally prohibitive. Memory systems offer a scalable alternative by compacting videos into text representations, but often fail on practical benchmarks: either the memory does not preserve key evidence, or the retriever fails to locate relevant entries due to retrieval competition in growing search spaces. To address these challenges, we introduce MemLife, a multimodal memory system that constructs entity-grounded, first-person text episodes and retrieves them via a time-indexed agentic reader. Without training or query-time video access, MemLife improves over the strongest training-free baseline by 4.6-12.0% across four long-horizon benchmarks. To further improve memory quality, we propose MemOpt, a reinforcement learning framework that optimizes the memory writer to produce faithful, informative, and retrievable memories. MemOpt consistently improves MemLife by 2.7-5.0% across different video and question distributions, with gains that generalize across writer and reader backbones and memory systems.

replace MatLoom: Layered Text-to-Material Generation in a Compact Program Space

Authors: Anson Y. Lam, Shuqing Li, Michael R. Lyu

Abstract: Material generation should produce not only an appearance, but also the rules that construct it. We introduce MatLoom, a compact, layer-oriented language for text-to-material generation with pretrained language models. Each program composes alpha-masked layers whose shared spatial expressions define coverage and physically based rendering (PBR) channels, making dependencies between patterns, color, and relief explicit. A standalone interpreter evaluates the program into material maps, while the source retains named fields and layer parameters for subsequent authoring. Without task-specific fine-tuning, our pipeline uses parser-guided repair and preview-based critique to revise material designs, then searches noise seeds while keeping each candidate's remaining source fixed. On a curated benchmark of 141 prompts evaluated with six backbones, our best-performing configuration achieves higher mean scores than three diffusion baselines on all four flat-layout prompt-alignment metrics, and the margin persists on seven of the eight metric-layout cells under feasible equal-budget selection including best-of-10 sampling with the same quick scorer, with staged CLIPScore a statistical tie. A per-metric oracle bound over the same ten samples, which requires test labels, narrows the judge margins to 6.1 points flat and a staged statistical tie. Its initial programs already exceed all three baselines on mean BLIPScore, before critique or seed search. Retained programs have a median length of 21 lines when pooled across backbones. In a blind four-way comparison involving 30 participants and 20 prompts, our renders receive 59.2% of choices, compared with 19.3% for the most-preferred baseline. Compact executable programs thus offer a way to generate prompt-aligned materials while retaining their construction as part of the asset.

replace Sphere Encoder 2

Authors: Kaiyu Yue, Sean McLeish, Ruchit Rawal, Brian Bartoldson, Menglin Jia, Tom Goldstein

Abstract: Sphere Encoder is an autoencoder that generates images by decoding random points from a high-dimensional latent sphere. We identify two limitations of the original formulation that reduce its generation quality. First, random points concentrate near the equator relative to the pole on an encoded latent, but the training rotation never reaches this region, leaving a gap that limits one-step generation. Second, training for generation with pixel-wise reconstruction loss encourages the decoder to average over plausible images, producing blurry images that lack high-frequency details. We present Sphere Encoder 2 to address both limitations, substantially improving image generation quality while maintaining the speed and simplicity of a autoencoder. Models are released at https://github.com/kaiyuyue/sphere2.

URLs: https://github.com/kaiyuyue/sphere2.

replace When MLLMs miss what humans read: diagnosing and learning to read squint text

Authors: Chen Peng, Shan Huang, Jianbo Huang, Linrui Qin, Qibo Sun, Mengwei Xu

Abstract: Multimodal LLMs can fail to read text embedded in an image's coarse structure, text a human recovers by squinting, and report that no text is present. We name this class Squint Text and study it as a recognition deficiency, motivated by cases from a production moderation pipeline. We introduce SquintBench, comprising 1076 production-derived moderation images, and SquintCtrl, a controlled synthetic suite for mechanism diagnosis. Frozen-weight analysis shows that hidden text leaves a coarse signal while glyph identity is weakly decodable under global pooling. Restricting read-out to the text region improves glyph-identity decoding, identifying spatial selection as an important factor in glyph-identity read-out; an exact glyph mask further improves decoding. Suppressing high-frequency content also improves reading at a fixed visual-token budget, while visual token count may also contribute. These findings motivate Squint OPSD: a frozen teacher receives a downscaled view and the ground-truth transcription as privileged context, and its token-level distributions supervise a student along on-policy trajectories. At inference, the student reads the original image alone. Squint OPSD reaches 88.61 ANLS on SquintBench, versus 26.50 for the unadapted backbone, 24.44 for the best general-purpose model on the original view, and 68.60 at best for an off-the-shelf model under a downscaled view. These results substantially narrow the gap between available visual evidence and its generated read-out without auxiliary views at inference.

replace FlashReFocus: Interactive Image Refocusing in Seconds

Authors: Ching-Heng Cheng, Chia-Ming Lee, Ming-Ching Chang, Xin Li, Chih-Chung Hsu

Abstract: Single-image refocusing changes the focal plane and depth of field of a photograph after capture. Users compare focal choices on one photograph, so content stability across edits matters alongside quality and speed. Recent generative methods rerun large diffusion models for every edit, even when they first restore an all-in-focus image, so content can change inconsistently between edits. Yet only this restoration is highly ill-posed and benefits from a generative prior, whereas rendering blur is largely determined by depth and the focal plane. We therefore propose FlashReFocus, which runs diffusion once per photograph and renders each edit with a lightweight network. A one-step diffusion deblurrer with a decoder refiner recovers fine detail efficiently while balancing fidelity and perceptual quality. The renderer uses an explicit defocus map for control and defocus-bias attention to promote information exchange between tokens with similar blur magnitudes. Because every edit reuses the same reconstruction, in-focus content stays consistent across edits, and editing the map enables tilt-shift and two-plane effects. We further collect 3CReal, a benchmark of 89 pairs from three camera-lens systems, to evaluate generalization to unseen optics. For zero-shot bokeh rendering on EBB! Val176 and 3CReal, FlashReFocus leads the evaluated methods in fidelity and perceptual quality. At 1536x1536, a complete refocus takes 2.45 s and each subsequent edit 0.46 s on one RTX PRO 6000.

replace WildAIGC-Bench: Can AI Image Detectors Handle the Wild?

Authors: Zijian Yu, Hangwei Zhang, Shunxing Chen, Jun Lan, Huijia Zhu, Weiqiang Wang, Jianfu Zhang

Abstract: An AI-generated scene or a subtle edit to a photograph can make a false claim about a public event appear credible. Existing benchmarks test detectors across generators and platforms, but offer limited insight into how well they recognize fabrications that circulate as evidence for such claims. We introduce WildAIGC-Bench, an event-driven benchmark designed for broad coverage, timely updates, and human-verified labels. A recurring search across news outlets, fact-checking sites, and social posts identifies emerging cases, and reviewers verify the evidence for each image. The benchmark contains 600 AI-generated or edited images and 638 genuine photographs of related subjects. It preserves posts alongside crops of the depicted images and links documented edits to their original photographs where available. All ten visual detectors achieve lower balanced accuracy on WildAIGC-Bench than their respective averages across eight external benchmarks with both classes. On 37\% of the AI images, at least three quarters of the evaluated detectors predict real. Cropping improves recall for most visual detectors but reduces it for most general-purpose multimodal models. Comparisons with editing originals further reveal local alterations that most detectors miss even when they correctly accept the original photograph.

replace LoRA Superposition across Pretrained Models

Authors: Keigo Watanabe, Hiroshi Kera, Kazuhiko Kawamoto

Abstract: Low-rank adaptation (LoRA) is usually tied to the pretrained backbone on which it is learned. We ask whether LoRAs trained independently for the same task on different pretrained backbones can be summed and applied unchanged to all contributing backbones. We call this construction LoRA superposition. Across seven ViT-B/16 CLIP backbones and eight image-classification tasks, the exact sum improves all 56 backbone-task pairs and achieves a mean accuracy gain of 30.21 percentage points over the unadapted backbone. The exact sum achieves a mean accuracy only 0.28 percentage points below that of individually trained solo LoRAs. In contrast, a leave-one-model-out (LOMO) sum produces a mean gain of only 0.06 percentage points over the unadapted backbones. Backbone-specific solo LoRAs are nearly orthogonal in parameter space, while pairwise cross-backbone responses are strongly concentrated on the diagonal. These results support a component-wise non-interference hypothesis, under which the frozen backbone acts as an implicit selector of its own component without explicit routing or cross-model alignment.

replace PosePlaner: Denoising Any Feed-Forward Pose Predictor from Pairwise Planar Geometry

Authors: Yihang Chen, Bin Tan, Yujun Shen, Yinghao Xu, Nan Xue

Abstract: Recent multi-view Transformers (e.g., DUSt3R and VGGT) have advanced 3D reconstruction by scaling training data and model capacity from two views to many views and long sequences, subsuming the classical SfM/SLAM pipeline of feature extraction, matching, and pose estimation in a single feed-forward pass. Yet this subsumption hides the interfaces between pipeline stages---correspondences, geometry, and pose are entangled in a single forward pass, leaving supervised fine-tuning as the major path to improvement and offering no mechanism to correct predictions from geometric evidence at test time. We propose PosePlaner to close this gap by reviving the classical coupling between correspondences and pose via flow matching, treating pose refinement as a conditional generation problem that takes coarse geometric matches and initial pose as input condition and fuses the resulting pose hypotheses into a single refined estimate, providing a continuous, data-driven analog of RANSAC voting. Our method is trained efficiently on two-view data, yet serves as a periodic refiner for streaming n-view pose estimators with no retraining. Empirically, it corrects failures of feed-forward predictors, substantially improves correspondence-based solvers, and reduces accumulated drift in long streaming sequences.

replace Any3R: Feedforward 3D Reconstruction from Any Camera Type

Authors: Jingong Chen, Qingwen Zhang, Youming Deng, Chulwoo Pack, Olaf Wysocki, Boris Jutzi, Kwanghee Won, Kyle Gao

Abstract: Geometry foundation models recover camera poses and dense scene geometry from multiple images using feed-forward networks, but are typically designed for pinhole cameras. This assumption is embedded in the backbone itself: positional encoding locates each patch by its pixel index and the training loss weights every pixel equally, so both treat image coordinates as if they fixed the viewing direction in 3D space, which holds only for pinhole cameras. We condition both on the dense ray field, which maps each pixel to its 3D viewing direction and represents any central camera regardless of its projection model. $\textit{Angular coordinates}$ use the viewing direction of each patch for positional encoding, and $\textit{solid-angle weighting}$ scales the point loss by the angular area covered by each pixel. Together, they allow the same encoding and supervision to be used across different camera models. $\textbf{Any3R}$ is therefore a feed-forward model that reconstructs from any mix of central cameras without lens-specific retraining. On a benchmark of eight central cameras across three datasets, where every method reconstructs with its own predicted camera poses, it is the most consistently accurate method. $\textbf{Any3R}$ enables 3D reconstruction from crowdsourced and archival image collections containing diverse camera types. Project page: https://jingong-chen.github.io/Any3R/

URLs: https://jingong-chen.github.io/Any3R/

replace Forget the Data, Keep the Capability: MoME, Switchable Unlearning for Retrieval

Authors: Jingong Chen, Kyle Gao, Guangyuan Li, Chulwoo Pack, Sijia Liu, Kwanghee Won

Abstract: Privacy compliance requires re-identification models to selectively forget individuals while preserving their ability to recognize others. For this, we present $\textbf{MoME}$, a switchable framework that can adapt pre-trained backbones for privacy compliance, allowing the same model to switch between $\textbf{Full}$ mode for standard inference and $\textbf{Core}$ mode for unlearned inference. MoME splits one backbone into $\textit{core parameters}$ that preserve retrieval and zero-initialized $\textit{parasitic parameters}$ that carry the full-data knowledge, and training them under $\textit{gradient isolation}$ so the core never receives a forget-set gradient. Disabling the branch yields $\textbf{Core}$, which satisfies a request to delete a person's training data, and enabling it yields $\textbf{Full}$, which retains what the deleted data taught, so honoring the request is a flag flip rather than a retraining run. We prove $\textbf{Core}$ independent of the forget set up to an audited set of coupling channels and introduce the rank-based $\textit{Forgetting-Rank}$ statistic to audit that independence in a trained model. On Market-1501, VeRi-776 and RegDB, $\textbf{Core}$ reads at a retrained reference under three membership attacks while retrieving above it, and the retrieval advantage carries to ResNet and CLIP backbones. Code and weights are available at https://github.com/Jingong-Chen/MoME

URLs: https://github.com/Jingong-Chen/MoME

replace GradSplat: Gradients Inside the Forward Pass for Feed-Forward 3D Gaussian Splatting

Authors: Jingong Chen, Linlian Jiang, Guangyuan Li, Xinxin Zuo, Yang Wang, Chulwoo Pack, Kyle Gao, Kwanghee Won

Abstract: Feed-forward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) reconstructs a scene from a few images in a single pass, yet per-scene optimization on the same images can still improve its predicted Gaussians substantially. We show that refining the Gaussian shapes (scales and rotations) recovers nearly all of this improvement. Building upon this finding, we propose $\textbf{GradSplat}$, a feed-forward model that performs gradient-conditioned Gaussian refinement within its forward pass, without requiring target views. It combines $\textit{log-anisotropy correction}$, which changes the aspect ratios of each Gaussian at constant volume, with $\textit{gradient-conditioned refinement}$, which feeds the gradient of the input-view loss to a learned recurrent update, first for opacity and color and then for the shape. GradSplat achieves the best PSNR and SSIM on RealEstate10K with input poses. Without input poses, it leads from $12$ views onward and, at six views, it only trails YoNoSplat, which, unlike GradSplat, requires ground-truth intrinsics. It also achieves the best results on DL3DV and the best PSNR and SSIM on ScanNet++ without fine-tuning. Project page: https://jingong-chen.github.io/GradSplat/

URLs: https://jingong-chen.github.io/GradSplat/

replace GenSight: Unified Video Generation and 4D Perception for Embodied World Modeling

Authors: Haitang Feng, Zhuoman Liu, Zhennan Wang, Sheng Wang, Juelin Li, Chengzhi Zhao, Hexiang Wei, Lin Shao

Abstract: Embodied world models should not only imagine plausible futures, but also perceive the geometric, semantic, and motion structure of the evolving world. We present GenSight, a unified embodied world model that brings future video generation and dense perception into a single pretrained video diffusion transformer. GenSight uses a unified spatiotemporal representation for video, depth, embodiment segmentation, and first-frame-relative 2D flow. Built on a shared DiT backbone, GenSight uses lightweight task modulation to predict all modalities within the same architecture. All tasks are jointly optimized within a unified velocity prediction space. At inference, GenSight supports two complementary modes: multi-step future video generation with aligned dense predictions and single-step dense perception from an observed video. By unifying modality representation, model architecture, and prediction space, GenSight avoids separate modality-specific pipelines and couples appearance, geometry, semantics, and motion in a shared representation. It promotes cross-modal consistency and allows dense perception to benefit from the spatiotemporal priors learned through video generation. Trained on embodied manipulation data spanning multiple robot embodiments and human-hand interactions, GenSight demonstrates strong performance in both video generation and dense 4D perception, highlighting the effectiveness of unifying seeing and imagining for embodied world modeling.

replace WorldFlow: Joint Video and 3D Motion Prediction via Large-Scale Pretraining

Authors: Yuhao Lu, Jiongxu Chen, Hexiang Wei, Jiajin Wu, Juelin Li, Mingjian Liang, Fan Yang, Zhuowei Liang, Zihao Liu, Zhennan Wang, Sheng Wang, Lin Shao

Abstract: Predicting future physical states stands as a central challenge in embodied intelligence. Existing video world models leave 3D dynamics implicit, action-based formulations remain tied to specific robot configurations, and 3D motion prediction models use flow-only supervision, providing a limited learning signal for physical dynamics. To circumvent these limitations, we introduce \method{}, a generative world model that jointly predicts future video and query-based 3D trajectories. By parameterizing 3D motion as continuous displacement tokens aligned with the video grid, a unified Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) backbone jointly denoises both modalities under a rectified-flow objective. This formulation couples explicit geometric dynamics with visual evolution, effectively unlocking the physical commonsense embedded within pretrained video models. To empirically scale and validate this unified architecture, we train \method{} on a heterogeneous mixture of 1.44 million object-level samples spanning simulated and real-world domains. We further introduce WorldFlowBench, comprising 1,678 evaluation samples from 11 data sources across simulated and real-world interactions, including spatial reasoning tasks. Across all three evaluation sets, WorldFlow achieves the lowest average displacement error (ADE) and final displacement error (FDE) among the evaluated learning-based methods. Notably, on the challenging SpatialCog set, WorldFlow achieves an ADE of 0.060 m, a 54.2\% reduction compared with the strongest external baseline. It also reduces ADE and FDE by 55.9\% and 68.5\%, respectively, compared with its flow-only variant. Project page: https://roboscience-ai.github.io/worldflow-page/.

URLs: https://roboscience-ai.github.io/worldflow-page/.

replace UA-Depth: Uncertainty-Aware Depth Restoration for Material-Agnostic Object Manipulation

Authors: Rui Cao, Jia-Hui Pan, Yin Zhang, Qiwei Meng, Jiapeng Li, Gang Wang, Runsong Zhu, Wei Chen, Chi-Wing Fu, Xieyuanli Chen, Jiangliu Wang, Yun-Hui Liu

Abstract: Robotic manipulation requires accurate metric depth, yet commodity RGB-D cameras often fail on non-Lambertian objects such as transparent, reflective, and translucent materials, producing holes, structured noise, and biased depth. Most restoration methods produce only a single depth map, treating noisy and reliable regions uniformly for downstream tasks while ignoring regional uncertainty. We present UA-Depth, an uncertainty-aware depth restoration framework that iteratively co-refines depth and pixel-wise uncertainty, and uses uncertainty-gated features to suppress unreliable cues for stable updates. To strengthen zero-shot behavior, UA-Depth leverages a frozen depth foundation model as a prior to initialize missing or unreliable depth, and applies two-stage MAD-weighted log-depth scale normalization to reduce outlier bias in relative-to-metric alignment. We introduce an eight-dataset zero-shot benchmark across diverse scenes, materials, and sensors. Across these benchmarks, UA-Depth achieves the best mean rank among representative baselines, with particularly strong gains on datasets dominated by transparent and reflective objects. Beyond depth metrics, we demonstrate downstream improvements in category-level 6D pose estimation and 6D grasp synthesis by exploiting the predicted uncertainty. \emph{Code, models, and data for training and evaluation will be released upon publication.}

replace dKV Forcing: Separating Generation and Memory Representations through a Differentiable KV Cache

Authors: Zhiqi Li, Bo Zhu

Abstract: A causal video generator writes every segment it produces into a key--value (KV) cache, and later segments know the past only through it. Self-rollout training stops the gradient at this cache, and Self Gradient Forcing (SGF) restores it, but applies it to the same weights that generate the current segment. We find that writing and generating are different roles: in a pretrained generator the written KV already differs from the KV used for denoising, and on shared weights the gradient from future reads is negatively aligned with the gradient of generation. We propose dKV Forcing (dKV denotes differentiable KV), which gives the write its own parameters, a low-rank residual used only when a segment enters the cache, and trains them with the gradient of the later segments that read what they wrote. Opening this gradient through the cache in common use exhausts GPU memory. We trace this to checkpoint replay re-writing the cache, not to the gradient path, which removes the need for the second reconstruction pass of SGF. dKV Forcing keeps the cache as persistent differentiable pages and computes the exact gradient in one temporal traversal, with an $11\%$ lower memory peak and $28\%$ less resident memory. With pages that store hidden states, it trains 15\,s clips on which the two-pass reconstruction runs out of memory. Trained on 5\,s clips, dKV Forcing reaches the highest VBench-Long quality score at 60\,s and 240\,s and outperforms SGF by $3.6$ and $6.1$ points of aesthetic quality, with a higher dynamic degree.

replace StepX-OPD: Rethinking Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation in Diffusion Models

Authors: Chenru Wang, Xuanyang Zhang, Yucheng Han, Wenbo Nie, Wei Cheng, Xianfang Zeng, Gang YU, Chi Zhang

Abstract: A deployable text-to-image diffusion model must hold several capabilities at once, such as instruction following, text rendering and human preference, and must sample in a few steps. Capabilities and few-step sampling are built in separate stages: a full-step model first consolidates capabilities through multi-teacher on-policy distillation, then serves as a fixed teacher for step distillation. Every full-step model we evaluate scores lower once compressed, and the second stage cannot begin until the first finishes. We introduce StepX-OPD, which merges the two stages: one student acquires every capability while learning to sample in a few steps, so frozen specialists supervise it on the states its own rollout visits rather than on a longer trajectory it never runs. Motivated by a decomposition of the rollout's KL, we also regress the student's velocity on the low-noise part of the rollout. Separately, we find that capability integration makes models write prompt words into images on prompts that never asked for text, and we give a variant that lowers it. We evaluate on SD3.5-Medium and FLUX.1-dev across instruction following, text rendering, image quality and human preference. On both architectures, StepX-OPD attains the highest normalized average score among the four-step methods compared and exceeds the forty-step Flow-OPD model, and on SD3.5-Medium it trains with about 37% fewer GPU-hours than sequential DiffusionOPD followed by step distillation.

replace EP-LVMQA: Evidence-Preserving Coarse-to-Fine Localization for Low-Quality Long Video Moment Question Answering

Authors: Guoning Chen, Zhexun Hu, Yifan Wei, Yixuan Weng, Bin Li, Yufei Zhao

Abstract: Long instructional videos are increasingly used in medical education, industrial training, and procedural knowledge transfer, yet their evidence is often incomplete or unreliable: subtitles may be missing, ASR noisy, OCR sparse, and decisive actions briefly shown without narration. Existing VideoQA systems typically return labels or free-form text, making answer support difficult to inspect. We study Long Video Moment Question Answering (LVMQA), where a system returns timestamped answer-supporting evidence. We propose EP-LVMQA, an evidence-preserving coarse-to-fine framework comprising an Uncertainty-Aware Multimodal Evidence Timeline, a Coarse Evidence Planner, and an Evidence-Conditioned Fine Localizer. An evidence card transfers planner scores, matched cues, source-confidence proxies, modality-specific evidence, neighboring context, and a temporal prior between the latter two modules. On MedVidQA, EP-LVMQA achieves 26.1 R1@0.5 and 31.8 mIoU, improving the strongest non-ours results by 6.3 and 5.1 points. It raises Suff.@2 and Ver.@2 to 25.4 and 22.6, gains of 9.5 and 8.8 points. Under matched low-quality perturbations, average relative degradation is reduced from the strongest non-ours value of 14.6% to 11.1%. On Ego4D-NLQ and QVHighlights, EP-LVMQA remains broadly comparable to established task-specific localization methods.

replace WAM-Sim0.1: World Anchored Memory Meets Online RL for Consistent and Realistic Driving Simulation

Authors: Jiahao Cui, Jiaye Li, Wenhao Zhang, Yaoyi Li, Shan Luan, Quanhui Tang, Jianhua Han, Lin Cheng, Hang Xu, Siyu Zhu

Abstract: Autoregressive driving world models systematically suffer from structural drift, identity drift, and quality decay over long rollout horizons due to transient conditioning signals that lack persistent state. We present WAM-Sim, a driving world model anchored by a persistent hierarchical memory and optimized via online flow-matching RL. WAM-Sim structures persistent context across three temporal and semantic scales: pose-aligned structural tokens encoding persistent road topology, read-only scene tokens supplying geolocated background appearance, and write-once instance tokens preserving individual traffic participant identities across occlusions and chunk boundaries. To restore visual sharpness and physical plausibility degraded by multi-scale conditioning, we introduce an online post-training framework that optimizes self-generated rollouts via flow-matching RL guided by hierarchy-aligned visual rewards and a vision-language model physical judge. The invariant memory empirically confines policy optimization within the anchored context, helping suppress semantic collapse and reward hacking, while online RL systematically enforces physical commonsense and fine-grained visual fidelity. On the nuScenes benchmark, WAM-Sim achieves a superior generation fidelity (FID=3.47, FVD=25.72) and strong downstream perception performance (24.16 mAP, 71.23 road mIoU). On minute-scale rollouts of self-collected data, WAM-Sim substantially mitigates structural and identity drift, preserving high scene similarity and subject identity coherence across extended horizons. Project page: https://fudan-generative-vision.github.io/WAM-Sim.

replace FreeOcc: Decoupling Ego-Motion for Efficient 4D Occupancy Forecasting via Continuous Flow Matching

Authors: Zeping Zhang, Zhuoya Zhao, Samy Metari, Robert Lagani\`ere

Abstract: Current 4D occupancy world models use heavy cross-attention or deep recurrence to disentangle ego-motion from scene dynamics. This requires significant computational capacity. However, this effort is largely redundant since the ego-vehicle's intended future motion is provided by the downstream motion planner. We present FreeOcc, a lightweight continuous-time world model that introduces explicit $SE(2)$ geometric decoupling into a conditional flow-matching framework. By mathematically factoring out self-motion, we create a purely dynamic feature space. This space supports attention-free temporal fusion and allows physics-constrained ODEs to operate strictly on residual agent kinematics. To maximize deployment efficiency, FreeOcc introduces an uncertainty-aware blending mechanism. This routes well-observed voxels to a single-pass decoder, reserving the ODE solver exclusively for ambiguous, high-uncertainty regions. Requiring only 28.3M parameters-roughly 40% of comparable models-our optimal ensemble mode (FreeOcc-E) runs at 27 FPS. On nuScenes, FreeOcc achieves state-of-the-art forecasting performance with a 0.47 m Near Field Chamfer Distance (NFCD) and 32.1% mIoU. Furthermore, the model transfers zero-shot to SemanticKITTI. It also proves highly robust, retaining 92.8% of its ideal mIoU under significant sensor packet loss and timestamp jitter.

replace Same Pixels, Different Documents: Measuring Part-Level Editability in Image Vectorization

Authors: Boheng Zhu, Zehua Wang, Wei Chen, Ming-Hao Lin

Abstract: Image vectorization is often evaluated by rendering fidelity, which cannot distinguish documents that render identically but support different edits. Edit-based evaluation tests outcomes, but an oracle that gathers a part's fragments can conceal the cost of selecting them. We introduce Pass@k, which tests whether a prescribed edit can succeed using objects selectable within k clicks under a specified interaction model. Eight document diagnostics characterize properties relevant to editing beyond these tasks. We assess structural sensitivity through controlled perturbations, practical relevance through designer operations, and reference dependence through alternative drawings. We evaluate twelve open and commercial methods on 211 references, with 635 edit tasks on 93 of them. In each of four reference sets, including two drawn by designers, 64-88% of oracle-passed tasks on parts composed of multiple shapes fail at Pass@1, against 6-40% for single shapes, pooling counts over configurations. Higher fidelity does not guarantee higher Pass@1, while selected diagnostics are associated with designers' effort in our study.

replace From Reflection to Co-Evolution: Self-Evolving Agents with Locally Verified Memory

Authors: Xinxiang Yin, Xinglang Zhang, Haodong Chen, Zhiqing Cui, Weidong Tang, Yuxuan Wang, Junrui Wang, Jingyi Zhang, Jianfeng Zhang, Jian Qian

Abstract: LLM agents have made impressive progress in coding and mathematical reasoning, motivating growing interest in learning from long-horizon interaction. Memory supports this process, but plausible lessons can be redundant or reinforce inappropriate actions. Can a memory's source decision provide a practical test for whether to retain it? We localize consequential trajectory steps, extract candidate memories, and replay each source decision with and without its candidate under the same context. This reveals two properties: most candidates barely shift the probed distribution, and both successful and failed source actions can be reinforced or suppressed. These observations motivate Anchor, which tests shift magnitude and source-role alignment before retention. Admitted memories guide later trajectories, enabling experience-driven improvement under a frozen policy. On held-out ALFWorld and WebShop tasks, Anchor improves success rate and mean score by up to 19.9% and 17.2%, respectively, relative to the strongest baseline for each backbone. With Qwen3.5-27B, it retains 41% fewer ALFWorld cards than unfiltered retention and outperforms all four WebShop cost-comparison baselines while using fewer tokens.

replace MARS: Multiscale Appearance-Residual Streams for Video Forgery Detection

Authors: Ahmed Y. Radwan, Shaina Raza

Abstract: Generalizing deepfake detection across facial manipulation and fully generated video remains challenging due to forgery traces varying with the manipulation method and scene content. Explicit spatial and temporal feature differences offer a way to capture this variation, but also respond to natural texture and motion, motivating their joint interpretation with appearance features. We investigate whether combining multiscale feature differences with adapted appearance representations improves detection across both forgery categories. We introduce Multiscale Appearance-Residual Streams (MARS), a two-path video detector. The residual path applies fixed spatial and temporal difference-of-Gaussians operators to frozen intermediate features and models the resulting differences alongside appearance context across frames. The semantic path adapts the encoder's normalization parameters for frame classification, and a clip-dependent gate combines their predictions. Under a shared protocol across seven datasets, MARS achieves the highest average AUROC of 91.43\%, outperforming seven compared detectors. When trained on facial manipulations alone, MARS achieves 93.99\% AUROC on GenVideo and 75.50\% on DF26, demonstrating transfer to generated videos without generated-video training data. MARS also provides signed patch and frame contributions, whose faithfulness we evaluate through controlled spatial and temporal removal against random controls. MARS is publicly available for research: Project page: https://vectorinstitute.github.io/mars/ Github: https://github.com/VectorInstitute/mars

replace MICRO: A Surgery-Phase Retinal Image Rigid Registration Benchmark and One-Step Frequency-Structure Similarity Registration Learning

Authors: Mingdao Gong, Yan Hu, Mingzhen Yuan, Jialu Zhang, Junming Feng, Yiming Jiang, Jing Ma, Jinghua Liu, Huazhu Fu, Hai Lu, Xiaoqing Zhang, Jiang Liu

Abstract: Precise multimodal retinal image rigid registration (MRIRG) is essential for aligning cross-modal vessel structures, offering critical guidance for ophthalmic surgery. Despite its significance, the advancement of MRIRG is hindered by the lack of publicly available multimodal retinal image rigid registration datasets spanning various ophthalmic surgery phases (e.g., preoperative, intraoperative, and postoperative). To address this issue, we introduce the MICRO dataset, a large-scale MRIRG benchmark, comprising 1,365 cross-modal retinal image pairs with standardized annotations across all ophthalmic surgery phases. Moreover, existing deep registration methods achieve competitive MRIRG results via a two-step segmentation-then-detection framework, but segmentation quality heavily constrains their performance. We argue that this dependency stems from an implicit need for keypoint topology constraints, which have not been exploited for MRIRG through a one-step registration framework. Inspired by the above analysis, we propose a novel One-step Frequency-Structure Similarity Registration (OFS2Reg) learning framework for MRIRG that leverages the potential of cross-modal vessel structure similarities to construct keypoint topology constraints under a shared detection network. To be specific, OFS2Reg applies a well-designed frequency-guided structure preservation descriptor to construct coarse keypoint topology constraints in descriptors by highlighting significant vessel structures. Additionally, we develop a new cross-modal vessel structure similarity learning method that refines cross-modal keypoint topology constraints to precisely align pixel-level cross-modal vessel structures by leveraging cross-modal vessel structure similarity priors.

Authors: Mahacine Ettahri, Hager Radi Abdelwahed, Toluwaleke Olutayo, Daniel Aloise, Ga\'etan Marceau Caron

Abstract: Wildfires represent a critical ecological and socio-economic challenge in Canada, where vast boreal forests and a warming climate have amplified the frequency and severity of extreme fire seasons. Predicting next-day wildfire spread can support fire management, but requires capturing complex interactions among weather, fuels, and terrain. Deep learning has emerged as a promising approach to learning these relationships from heterogeneous environmental data, yet its development and evaluation depend on representative datasets. Existing deep learning research on wildfire spread relies predominantly on US-focused datasets, leaving Canadian fire environments underrepresented. To address this gap, we present DailyWildfireCAN, a multi-modal, multi-temporal dataset and benchmark for predicting next-day wildfire spread across Canada. The dataset tracks continuous fire progression over multi-day events at a high spatial and temporal resolution of 90-m and 24-hour, respectively. This resolution preserves fine-scale information relevant to fire-edge geometry, local fuel variations, and terrain effects, supporting research toward improved daily wildfire spread forecasting. DailyWildfireCAN covers 2,440 wildfire events across eight fire seasons 2017-2024 and integrates 33 features across diverse modalities, including topography, meteorology, land cover, vegetation and forest structure, human influence indices, and satellite imagery. We establish a comprehensive benchmark comparing mono-temporal and multi-temporal prediction models, accompanied by a multi-dimensional evaluation framework assessing robustness across wildfire characteristics, geographic regions, and temporal conditions, spatial prediction quality, and sensitivity to reference label variations.

replace Toward Cross-Rubric Surgical Skill Assessment via Joint Embedding Prediction

Authors: Jiajun Cheng, Haidar M. Abdul-Muhsin, Juha Choi, Shan Lin

Abstract: Expert review of surgical videos is essential for surgical training but labor-intensive, motivating AI-assisted skill assessment to provide feedback at scale. Although surgical assessment rubrics may use different language to describe similar skills, existing automated methods commonly predict predefined skill levels or numerical scores tied to fixed rubrics, limiting their ability to transfer across semantically equivalent assessment criteria. We propose Predictive Rubric Alignment (PRA), a joint embedding prediction approach that scores surgical videos using natural-language descriptions of skill levels. Rather than matching level probabilities, PRA predicts a rater-weighted combination of skill-level description embeddings. Adjacent description features define a score-parameterized path, and the predicted embedding is projected onto this path to read the score. We introduce two variants: \textit{PRA-item}, which uses learned queries for predefined assessment items, and \textit{PRA-cond}, which generates queries from textual item names to enable assessment of unseen items without retraining. On AIxSuture, a dataset of expert-rated suturing videos, PRA outperforms published baselines in skill classification. We further evaluate zero-shot transfer to the smaller surgical skill assessment benchmark JIGSAWS. Trained on only two AIxSuture rubric items, PRA-cond assesses unseen JIGSAWS items without further training, yielding positive score correlations and greater Grad-CAM overlap with instruments and suture than a description-matching baseline. These results support predictive rubric alignment as a promising direction for flexible and explainable automated surgical skill assessment.

replace Raysemble: Metric 3D Bone Reconstruction from a Single X-ray

Authors: Xiaoyang Li, Xinghao Yan, Yuan Chai

Abstract: Raysemble reconstructs eight lumbar and pelvic bones from a single X-ray by separating shape and placement. Each 3D query samples regional and global features at its image projection. A shared decoder predicts normalized bone shapes. Predicted depth and camera geometry determine 3D centers and scales for resampling these shapes onto a common grid in millimeters. Evaluation uses 23 held-out patients with 43 CT scans, each generating one simulated anteroposterior radiograph. Without rigid alignment to CT,Raysemble leads 15 evaluated methods, averaging 65.24% Dice, 9.09 mm HD95, and 3.32 mm ASSD over three random seeds. replace DRA-HTR: Disentangling Representation and Adaptation for Handwritten Text Recognition

Authors: Gayan H. Pathirage, Florent Imbert, Denis Coquenet, Elisa H. Barney Smith

Abstract: Handwritten text recognition (HTR), particularly when applied to historical documents, remains challenging due to the scarcity of annotated data and the substantial domain shifts caused by variations in handwriting styles, document degradation, acquisition conditions, and linguistic characteristics. While large amounts of unlabeled target-domain data are often available, effectively exploiting them requires adaptation strategies tailored to the specific requirements of HTR. We propose DRA-HTR, an unsupervised generative domain adaptation pipeline specifically designed for HTR and built upon the DRANet framework. The proposed approach combines source-domain pretraining, unsupervised adaptation to the visual characteristics of the target domain, relying on the generation of target-style source samples for task-specific training. We further investigate the integration of post-HTR correction to complement visual adaptation with language adaptation, relying on large language models, which we introduce as DRAL-HTR. The proposed framework is evaluated across seven HTR datasets spanning four languages, using a single set of hyperparameters for all source-target combinations. The recognition model achieves state-of-the-art performance on four datasets and competitive results on the remaining three. In addition, the proposed DRA-HTR establishes a new state-of-the-art for domain adaptation across all seven datasets and substantially improves overall robustness and training stability compared to the original DRANet framework: it reduces the Character Error Rate (CER) in average by 13.52 percentage points, and the average standard deviation decreases from 7.23 to 1.11 percentage points. Finally, post-HTR correction further reduces the CER by 2.16 percentage points. Code is available at https://github.com/FIorentI/DRA-HTR-Disentangling-Representation-and-Adaptation-for-Handwritten-Text-Recognition.

replace Pros4D: Modeling Evolving 4D Worlds in Latent Space for Dynamic Spatial Reasoning

Authors: Rongxin Gao, Dongxuan Liu, Chu Li, Chengyang Ji, Jialing Huang, Runyu Chen, Kairun Wen, Chenxin Li, Jingrui Ye, Haosong Zhen, Xinghao Ding, Yue Huang, Yuzhi Huang, Xiaotong Tu, Jingyan Jiang

Abstract: Dynamic spatial reasoning from monocular video enables vision-language models (VLMs) to understand how 3D environments evolve over time, supporting physical world understanding and embodied intelligence. Existing efforts primarily address observed-dynamics understanding, whereas agents in dynamic environments also require future-dynamics prediction beyond the observation boundary. Moreover, external geometric processing and textual descriptions can increase inference complexity or lose information when representing continuous dynamics. These limitations motivate a shared internal state that preserves observed dynamics and remains predictive beyond the input video. Accordingly, we present Pros4D, a framework for modeling evolving 4D worlds in latent space that unifies retrospective understanding of observed-dynamics with prospective reasoning for future-dynamics prediction. Specifically, Pros4D-Engine derives geometry-grounded supervision and constructs paired examples for both tasks, while Pros4D-Bench evaluates them across eight spatial task categories spanning dynamic cameras, objects, and scenes. At the model level, Pros4D compresses observed dynamics into latent observed states and autoregressively rolls them forward into latent future states. 4D Latent Alignment (4DLA) grounds these states through complementary visual-semantic and geometric supervision, while joint language supervision connects them to task reasoning. Experiments across benchmarks show consistent improvements over strong baselines in both capabilities.

replace SRED: Sparse Regional Evidence Distillation Across Magnifications in Whole-Slide Imaging

Authors: Zhiyuan Yang, Jiahao Cheng, Vincent Quoc-Huy Trinh, Mahdi S. Hosseini

Abstract: In computational pathology, high-magnification whole-slide image analysis preserves tissue detail but requires encoding many patches, whereas lower magnification reduces computation by covering a higher field of view per patch while sacrificing detail. Existing cross-magnification distillation matches features that often encode detail unreachable by low-magnification input, sometimes requiring slide-level labels that limit scalability. To address those issues, we introduce Sparse Regional Evidence Distillation (SRED), which transfers regional event occurrences that encode reachable high-level interactions between details without slide-level labels. We use a sparse autoencoder (SAE) to learn an event dictionary from 20x teacher features. Logical OR pooling records whether each event is detected in any spatially aligned patch, retaining both localized and shared evidence within the corresponding 5x region. A pretrained 5x encoder is fine-tuned to predict these regional targets without slide-level labels. Visual analysis identifies dictionary components responsive to recognizable histological patterns, supporting target interpretation. Compared with native 5x features, SRED improves average macro-F1 by 1.1 points across seven classification and grading cohorts and average C-index by 1.7 points across five survival cohorts. Runtime matches native 5x, with an average 11.58x speedup over 20x.

replace PCRG: Training-Free Pruning and Caching of Reference Tokens for Video Generation

Authors: Siyuan Wang, Haoran Li, Yi Yue, Tak L. Fung, Quan Chen

Abstract: Reference-to-video diffusion transformers pack the variational autoencoder (VAE) latents of all references and the generated tokens into one full-attention sequence, in which reference tokens make up about half. Because attention cost grows quadratically with sequence length, these tokens take up an even larger share of the attention computation, yet most of them are static, smooth or irrelevant to the output. To accelerate reference-conditioned generation, we propose pruning and caching of reference tokens for video generation (PCRG), a training-free, plug-and-play method that uses two signals the model already computes to assign each reference token to one of three tiers, namely pruned, cached or kept. The first is a low-level redundancy r from VAE latents, since a token whose latent barely changes over time or across its neighbors adds little new content. The second is a high-level importance g from the vision-language-model (VLM) conditioner, where the attention that the prompt and any first or last frames pay to a token tells whether the output needs to read it. Tokens that are not redundant are kept, and redundant tokens are cached if important and pruned otherwise. Pruned tokens leave the sequence before the first step with all rotary position embedding positions unchanged. Cached tokens have their keys and values computed once and reused, and kept tokens are processed as usual. On MiniMax-H3, already distilled to 8 timesteps and quantized to int8, PCRG is 1.57x faster end to end on our V2V-Bench and 1.49x faster on the public VACE-Benchmark, while the quality of VBench drops by only 0.24% and 0.17%, respectively. PCRG shortens the sequence rather than changing how attention is computed or which steps are run, so it also stacks on step caching and sparse attention. On V2V-Bench it reaches 1.86x with TeaCache, 1.82x with Spark at nearly unchanged fidelity and 2.15x with Veda.

replace Event-Based Person Detection for Maritime UAV Search

Authors: Zijie Ning, Patrick Vandewalle

Abstract: Maritime search and rescue requires locating people in the water quickly, motivating the use of UAVs to survey the search area. During flight, camera motion and the longer exposures used in low light can blur small targets and hinder person detection. Event cameras record brightness changes as they occur, reducing the motion blur associated with longer exposure times. We investigate their use for maritime person detection by generating synthetic events using the v2e event simulator from public MOBDrone rescue videos and retaining the existing annotations. We compare the recurrent vision transformer model RVT-T with YOLOv8n and RT-DETR-R18 trained on exposure-matched images. On 843 test samples from held-out recordings, RVT-T achieves 15.63% person AP50:95, compared with 6.89% for YOLOv8n and 9.44% for RT-DETR-R18 under 100 ms synthetic exposure. Increasing exposure times from 50 to 200 ms progressively reduces frame-based detection accuracy, widening the gap to the event model. To evaluate the feasibility of on-board processing, we deploy our detector on a Jetson Orin Nano platform. The RVT-T model retains its accuracy and requires approximately 136 ms and 2.02 J per sample, allowing real-time processing on the UAV.

replace EviSlide-MIL: Multi-Query Pooling and Shared Prediction for Class-Incremental Whole-Slide Image Classification

Authors: Zhouhan Qian, Sicheng Chen, Chad Wong, Tianyi Zhang, Enhui Chai, Zeyu Liu, Fei Xia

Abstract: Histopathological analysis of whole-slide images (WSIs) plays a critical role in cancer diagnosis, and multiple instance learning (MIL) is widely adopted for WSI analysis under weak supervision. As new tumor subtypes are introduced, extending existing MIL models requires learning new classes while retaining knowledge of previously learned ones. This presents a class-incremental learning challenge, where models must acquire new classes with limited access to historical training data and recognize all learned classes without task identifiers at inference. A major obstacle is forgetting, as updates to feature aggregation and prediction modules can degrade performance on previously learned classes. To address this challenge, we propose \textbf{EviSlide-MIL}, a class-incremental WSI framework that integrates multiple learnable queries per class with a shared prediction module. The queries aggregate frozen patch features into class-specific representations, while the shared prediction module limits parameter growth across incremental stages. By freezing queries associated with previously learned classes, EviSlide-MIL preserves their attention patterns for identical inputs, while experience replay and knowledge distillation further mitigate forgetting as shared parameters are updated. Experiments on eight tumor subtypes from The Cancer Genome Atlas demonstrate that EviSlide-MIL achieves improved performance and reduced forgetting compared with competing methods across most learning settings, demonstrating its potential for scalable and knowledge-preserving WSI classification. Code and pretrained models will be released.

replace Autoregressive View Model

Authors: Jensen (Jinghao), Zhou, Samuel Rota Bulo, Denis Rozumny, Duncan Zauss, Peter Hedman, Nikhil Keetha, Philip Torr, Christian Rupprecht, Andrea Vedaldi, Peter Kontschieder

Abstract: We present the Autoregressive View Model (AVM), which generates a scene as a stream of views without constructing an explicit 3D representation. Through a unified in-context interface, AVM conditions on images or videos to generate short clips along a requested camera path. Visual context grounds reconstruction and interpolation, while a learned prior enables extrapolation into unseen regions. AVM retrieves relevant views from inputs and generated history to form a bounded active context. We fine-tune a pretrained bidirectional video diffusion transformer with flow matching in an N-in-1-out formulation. We then tune a causal model on its own rollouts to improve photorealism, reduce denoising steps, and limit long-horizon drift. We scale training to approximately 8M scenes and video clips. We evaluate generation capacity and scene persistence across benchmarks for camera control, text control, visual quality, dynamics, sparse and 4D reconstruction, and long-horizon consistency. The results demonstrate that AVM adapts to diverse input and output settings through one in-context interface.

replace RoughCut-Bench: A Benchmark for Automatic Video Rough-Cut with Large Vision-Language Models

Authors: Ruisi Zhao, You Li, Sihan Chen, Aosong Cheng, Chixiang Ma, Sen Pei, Zhen Yang, Hehe Fan, Dongliang He, Yi Yang

Abstract: Video rough-cut is a core stage in media production workflows, organizing raw media materials into an initial video cut. Despite recent advances in large vision-language models (LVLMs) for video-related tasks, automatic rough-cut remains underexplored, without a comprehensive benchmark for evaluation or mature workflows for producing executable cut plans. To address the evaluation gap, we introduce RoughCut-Bench, which covers two practical settings: (1) a visually driven setting over video cases, covering highlight detection and selection, source-video ordering, and adherence to user requirements when provided; and (2) a speech-driven setting over automatic speech recognition (ASR) transcripts, covering filler removal, expression deduplication, and low-value content trimming. Across these settings, the benchmark contains 101 visually driven cases, including 831 raw videos with 1,476 highlight annotations. It also includes 84 speech-driven cases, each consisting of an ASR transcript with annotated cleanup spans. The evaluation compares cut plans with these annotations in both settings and additionally uses a human-aligned agentic evaluator to assess rendered-cut quality in the visual setting. To apply LVLMs to automatic rough-cut, we further propose agentic workflows that guide them to produce structured cut plans from input materials. Experiments show that current LVLMs remain limited in producing expert-aligned cut plans. Our benchmark, code, and prompts are released at https://whalesong-zrs.github.io/RoughCut-Bench-Project-Page.

URLs: https://whalesong-zrs.github.io/RoughCut-Bench-Project-Page.

replace Do VLMs Need to Keep Looking at Long Videos? Write It Down, Then Look Away

Authors: Pengyu Li, Zhitao Gao, Lingling Zhang, Heng Wang, Chengqian Ma, WenHao Wang, Wenxuan Huang, Chen Zheng, Jun Liu

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) reason about long videos over hundreds to thousands of tokens. Every decoding step re-reads the key-value (KV) cache of the entire video. Must the model keep looking while it reasons? In LookAway, the model first writes timestamped notes on what the question needs and then puts the video away. Once the notes close, the inference engine hides the video's KV cache for the rest of the response. Across both hybrid and pure-attention VLMs, dependence on the video is concentrated early in the response. On Qwen3.6-27B, writing notes preserves accuracy better than free reasoning at the same withdrawal step. Replacing them with another video's notes harms accuracy, whereas keeping the video visible alongside the model's own notes adds no significant benefit. Without additional training, LookAway nearly matches full-video thinking accuracy at 0.12-0.13 times its modeled decode cost. To shorten this initial look, we fine-tune the model on its own correct LookAway responses. Reinforcement learning then rewards correct answers while penalizing KV reads. The trained model puts the video away after a median of 66-68 tokens. Across three held-out benchmarks, it writes more tokens than an identically trained full-video model yet reads only 27-35% as many KV positions at matched accuracy.

replace SCRiD: Spatial and Contextual References for Generalizable AI-Generated Video Detection

Authors: Hao Tan, Zekai Zuo, Boyang Chen, Jinghui Qin

Abstract: Generalizable AI-generated video detection remains challenging because natural motion and content variations can resemble synthesis artifacts, making isolated video discrepancies unreliable indicators of authenticity. To address this challenge, we propose Spatial and Contextual Reference-informed Detection (SCRiD), a framework that interprets video variations through complementary spatial and contextual references. Specifically, we introduce Matched-Coordinate Residual Response (MCR) to characterize temporal residual associations under motion and coordinate references with matched subpixel sampling conditions. We further develop Real-Conditioned Score Adaptation (RCSA), which learns a content-conditioned denoising reference from authentic videos and probes its responses across multiple noise scales and quality views to guide feature adaptation. To integrate these complementary sources of evidence, we design Reversible Evidence Exchange (REE) to enable information exchange through reversible symplectic updates. We train SCRiD using synthetic videos from only 4 generators and evaluate it across 88 generator or task subsets spanning 6 benchmarks. Our method achieves average ACC and AP of 72.72% and 74.23%, respectively, outperforming the strongest baseline by 3.24 and 0.81 percentage points. Ablation and robustness experiments further validate the effectiveness of the proposed components and the stability of SCRiD under common post-processing operations.

replace Jeveration: System One Models Now Generate

Authors: Yuhe Liu, Qi Li, Shihua Zhang, Xinchao Wang

Abstract: System One models such as Jev return decisions through fast, parallel prediction. In this paper, we introduce Jeveration, showing that the same decision paradigm can also generate high-quality images in a single forward pass. Jeveration builds on a frozen vision-language model, repurposing its visual-semantic knowledge for holistic image generation. The key challenge is to maintain global consistency. A single prompt admits many valid images, yet parallel predictions must converge on a coherent whole. We therefore condition all positions on a shared random state to coordinate parallel predictions, so that each draw yields a different but coherent image. Since the model tends to ignore this state under direct image supervision, we first learn a distribution over discrete image codes to guide single-pass generation. Beyond text-to-image generation, we further unify image-to-image generation and dense prediction within the same architecture, so that one checkpoint supports all three tasks, each in one forward pass. Experiments show that Jeveration generates a 512x512 image in 78 ms, up to 122x faster than existing unified models, while maintaining competitive image quality and dense prediction performance.

replace Fold the Loop: Decoding-Time KV Memory for Looped Vision-Language Models

Authors: Kun Zhou, Wenmian Yang, Zhensheng Wang, Yanzeng Li, Weijia Jia

Abstract: Looped vision-language models (VLMs) deepen visual reasoning by repeatedly applying weight-tied modules. However, their decoding memory grows with recurrent depth and limits the image size and batch size that fit in a fixed memory budget. Each recurrent step produces its own key-value (KV) states, so the cache holds several copies of the same image. We find that, after an exact prefill, earlier steps still need access to visual information but can reuse the final visual KV within the same module. We propose FoldCache, which compresses visual KV along the recurrent axis while preserving the full prefill computation. FoldCache partitions recurrent steps within each module into groups and retains the final visual KV of each group for its members to share during decoding. Sharing stays within module boundaries, while question and answer KV remain separate for each step. The grouping granularity controls the degree of compression, and folding can be combined with token eviction and quantization to further reduce memory use. On LoopVL, FoldCache approximately halves prefix KV memory while matching full-cache accuracy on five open-ended benchmarks. These memory savings support batch sizes up to 1.7 times those of a full cache under the same GPU memory budget, and up to 2.6 times with the more aggressive FoldCache-Lite.

replace Text Lives in the Guidance: Preventing Silent Text Rewriting in Cached Diffusion Transformers

Authors: Kun Zhou, Wenmian Yang, Zhensheng Wang, Weijia Jia

Abstract: Diffusion transformers (DiTs) now render long, legible text in images, and feature caching is a standard way to accelerate them. Caches are judged by image-level metrics, which do not check whether the rendered text is still correct. We find that caching silently rewrites this text. On Z-Image, TaylorSeer at 2.9x fewer FLOPs lowers word accuracy from 62% to 46%, while ImageReward and CLIP Score barely change. Sampling with fewer steps at the same compute keeps almost all of this text, so the loss comes from how the cache predicts the skipped steps. Decomposing the cache error along classifier-free guidance (CFG), we trace this loss mainly to the guidance term, the difference between the conditional and unconditional predictions, which caches extrapolate worst on text. Correcting the guidance inside the text alone recovers most of the lost text. We therefore propose GuideCache, which recomputes both CFG branches only on the tokens with the largest predicted guidance error, using the guidance terms that the cache already stores. At 2.4x fewer FLOPs, GuideCache restores full-compute prompt fidelity on Z-Image and Qwen-Image, and on Z-Image it renders text as well as or better than ToCa, TeaCache, and FasterCache while staying much closer to the full model's output. GuideCache works with caches of any order, and on top of fewer sampling steps it keeps more text than step reduction at a higher speedup. These results suggest that acceleration methods for text-rendering models should be evaluated on the text they render.

replace Decision Boundaries Matter: Graph-Regularized Residual Adaptation for Cross-Domain Fake Image Detection

Authors: Shichuang Xie, Qishuai Ding, Tong Qiao, Liquan Shen, Guorui Feng

Abstract: Despite learning transferable features, fake image detectors may struggle on unseen domains due to mismatched decision boundaries. Our analysis shows that features extracted by the frozen forensic encoder remain discriminative, but the source-trained decision boundary does not fully exploit this information. However, correcting this boundary without source data or target labels remains challenging, as unreliable source predictions may lead to incorrect adaptation. To address this issue, we propose Graph-Regularized Residual Adaptation (GraRA), which refines the source decision boundary using only unlabeled target data. To determine which classifier directions to update, GraRA constructs a compact residual subspace from uncertainty-weighted target features and keeps it orthogonal to the source classification direction. To guide these updates, it uses source-score anchors to generate soft labels through target-graph diffusion, while neighborhood consistency regularizes residual learning within the constrained subspace. The learned correction is merged into the source classifier, requiring only 32 optimized coefficients and no additional inference branches. On diverse unseen OpenMMSec domains, GraRA achieves 84.36% accuracy and 91.34% AUC, improving over the source detector by 4.33 and 3.50 percentage points, respectively. Further analysis shows that reliable source predictions and locally consistent neighborhoods help guide residual learning, allowing GraRA to correct ranking errors that a simple threshold shift cannot address.

replace MIR-Depth: Multi-Range Refinement for Single-View RGB-D Depth Completion of Transparent Objects

Authors: Xinzhe Fu, Qi Shao, Yuanfei Xie, Taoyu Wu, Zhihao Cao, Long Hu, Hong Liang, Xianzhi Li, Anh Nguyen, Baoru Huang, Rongsheng Li

Abstract: Recovering accurate visible-surface depth from a single RGB-D image and an object mask is essential for robotic grasping and manipulation of transparent objects. Specular reflections cause missing depth, while refraction corrupts nonzero returns with background depth, making raw measurements unreliable as surface anchors. Under a fixed sampling budget, refining an initial single-view prediction within a single search interval either misses large refraction offsets or sacrifices fine local resolution. To resolve this single-view trade-off, MIR-Depth constructs Coordinate-Aware Multi-Range Hypotheses: a 3D-aggregated global inverse-depth volume and depth-axis-filtered pixel-wise local volumes around a prior-guided initial depth. Selective Recurrent Refinement then introduces out-of-bounds lookup masking and validity-masked softmax fusion to iteratively update inverse depth from these volumes using a multi-scale ConvGRU. Finally, Spatially Gated Correction applies a learned per-pixel spatial gate to the full-resolution residual, preserving accurate initial geometry without imposing raw sensor readings as hard constraints. Experiments on TransCG, TODD, TRansPose, ClearPose, and HouseCat6D show that MIR-Depth achieves lower error than ReMake, DFNet, BP-Net, and PSD. Real-robot evaluations confirm accurate surface geometry for 6-DoF grasp pose prediction. Code and pretrained models will be publicly available.

replace Carry the Fine Print: Budget-Aware Visual Token Compression for Document OCR

Authors: Yixuan Huang, ShengFan Zhang, Jiawei Chen

Abstract: Visual token compression often treats salience or similarity as a proxy for transcription importance. Complete document OCR breaks this assumption: small symbols and repeated entries can be indispensable to the transcript despite being visually inconspicuous. We present Select and Compensate, a two-stage framework built on a simple idea: choosing where to retain a token need not determine all the evidence it carries. Selection combines adaptive encoding resolution with global importance and local contrast to choose which tokens to retain as anchors. Compensation enriches these anchors with nearby discarded content and its relative position, without adding tokens. The OCR backbone remains frozen, and native coordinates and structural tokens are preserved. We evaluate complete page transcription across five backbones on OmniDocBench and document reconstruction on MPDocBench. With half the original visual tokens, our input-adapted configuration reaches 95.42 OmniDocBench Overall on Ovis versus 96.09 for the uncompressed model, and paired evaluations show gains over simply reducing the input resolution on both benchmarks. With the original input grid, our method stays within 0.17 points of the uncompressed model at 75% retention and exceeds a learned global selector by 4.70 points at 25%. On a fixed 200-page Ovis workload at the same 50% visual-token budget, only 3 of its outputs hit the length limit, versus 30-34 for the evaluated RTPrune, DivPrune, and HiPrune adaptations, and it finishes the batch 35-40% sooner. Code will be made publicly available.

replace Streaming-AAA: Streaming AAA Game Video Generation at High Resolution, High Frame Rate, and Low Latency

Authors: Zhihao Hu, Jianqi Ma, Jun Zhang, Jiayang Bai, Haifeng Shi, Yanxiang Zhuang, Zhongqian Sun, Weidong Zhang, Wei Yang

Abstract: Controllable streaming video generation offers a promising route toward interactive game simulation, yet existing methods remain far from practical gameplay. They often fail to jointly achieve high visual fidelity, high frame rates, and low interaction latency, resulting in poor responsiveness and degraded user experience. We present Streaming-AAA, a new paradigm for controllable infinite generation of AAA-quality game videos. Our system generates 1080p gameplay videos at 43 FPS with 198 ms end-to-end latency interaction on merely NVIDIA A800 GPUs, while supporting long-horizon play without severe temporal degradation or collapse. Streaming-AAA is built upon three key designs. First, we redesign the VAE architecture for efficient high-resolution decoding, enabling real-time synthesis at 1080p. Second, we pretrain the diffusion model using diffusion forcing and significantly extend the temporal training horizon, allowing the model to capture long-term dependencies and maintain persistent memory. Third, we perform post-training distillation and memory compression to improve inference efficiency while retaining visual quality and temporal consistency. Experiments show that Streaming-AAA substantially improves visual fidelity, frame rate, latency, and long-term stability over prior controllable video generation approaches, enabling a significantly more playable experience for interactive AAA game video generation.

replace-cross Zero-shot Video Moment Retrieval via Off-the-shelf Multimodal Large Language Models

Authors: Yifang Xu, Yunzhuo Sun, Benxiang Zhai, Ming Li, Wenxin Liang, Yang Li, Sidan Du

Abstract: The target of video moment retrieval (VMR) is predicting temporal spans within a video that semantically match a given linguistic query. Existing VMR methods based on multimodal large language models (MLLMs) overly rely on expensive high-quality datasets and time-consuming fine-tuning. Although some recent studies introduce a zero-shot setting to avoid fine-tuning, they overlook inherent language bias in the query, leading to erroneous localization. To tackle the aforementioned challenges, this paper proposes Moment-GPT, a tuning-free pipeline for zero-shot VMR utilizing frozen MLLMs. Specifically, we first employ LLaMA-3 to correct and rephrase the query to mitigate language bias. Subsequently, we design a span generator combined with MiniGPT-v2 to produce candidate spans adaptively. Finally, to leverage the video comprehension capabilities of MLLMs, we apply VideoChatGPT and span scorer to select the most appropriate spans. Our proposed method substantially outperforms the state-ofthe-art MLLM-based and zero-shot models on several public datasets, including QVHighlights, ActivityNet-Captions, and Charades-STA.

replace-cross Adaptive Lipschitz-Free Proximal Gradient Methods for Stochastic Composite Nonconvex Optimization

Authors: Ganzhao Yuan

Abstract: We propose {\sf ALFPG}, an Adaptive Lipschitz-Free Proximal Gradient framework for stochastic composite nonconvex minimization. It adapts stepsizes from the optimization trajectory without requiring smoothness constants or line search. For finite-sum problems, {\sf ALFPG-FS} combines coordinate-wise stepsizes, Nesterov extrapolation, and a SPIDER estimator; {\sf ALFPG-D} is its full-batch specialization. Under component-wise smoothness, bounded sublevel sets, and separate lower bounds on $f$ and $h$, {\sf ALFPG-FS} achieves expected $\epsilon$-stationarity with $\mathcal O(N+\sqrt N \epsilon^{-2})$ component-gradient evaluations. For expectation problems, we develop {\sf ALFPG-MVR1} and {\sf ALFPG-MVR2} (MVR: Momentum-based Variance Reduction). Both use one fresh sample per iteration. MVR1 uses one stochastic-gradient evaluation; MVR2 uses at most two evaluations at different points with the same sample. MVR1 requires smoothness of the expected loss; MVR2 additionally requires uniformly smooth sample losses. When the regularizer is convex or Lipschitz continuous on its effective domain and stochastic gradients are almost surely bounded, both methods achieve expected $\epsilon$-stationarity using $\widetilde{\mathcal O}(\sigma^2\epsilon^{-4}+\epsilon^{-2})$ and $\widetilde{\mathcal O}(\sigma\epsilon^{-3}+\epsilon^{-2})$ stochastic-gradient evaluations, respectively. Here, $N$ is the number of component functions, $\sigma^2$ bounds the gradient noise variance, and $\widetilde{\mathcal O}$ hides polylogarithmic factors. Neither expectation variant requires prior knowledge of $\sigma$, and both recover $\widetilde{\mathcal O}(\epsilon^{-2})$ complexity in the zero-noise regime. Numerical experiments on sparse phase retrieval, linear eigenvalue problems, and neural network regression show that all four ALFPG variants generally outperform state-of-the-art proximal-gradient baselines.

replace-cross Learning to Assimilate: Amortized Control for Autoregressive Diffusion

Authors: Prakhar Srivastava, Farrin Marouf Sofian, Francesco Immorlano, Kushagra Pandey, Stephan Mandt

Abstract: Autoregressive diffusion models (ARDMs) offer expressive priors for forecasting chaotic dynamics. However, maintaining consistency with sparse observations over long rollouts can require substantial test-time computation. We introduce Control-Augmented Data Assimilation (CADA), which trains a lightweight controller on top of a frozen ARDM to steer predictions toward future observations. Using a KL-regularized stochastic optimal control objective, the controller learns from short autoregressive rollouts to anticipate observations while staying close to the pretrained dynamics. At inference, it injects corrections at each denoising step without per-forecast optimization. Across two chaotic PDE benchmarks, CADA improves long-horizon accuracy, stability, and physical fidelity under sparse observations. On compact ERA5, it outperforms diffusion-based baselines in most regimes with over $10\times$ faster inference than conditional diffusion baselines. Applied to frozen GenCast, CADA also reduces temperature errors across 12--48-hour forecasts with spatially sparse observations. Code is available at https://github.com/mandt-lab/cada.
