replace-cross Mega-MMDF and DeepfakeBench-MM: Benchmarking Multimodal Deepfake Detection Across Diverse Forgery Pipelines

Authors: Kangran Zhao, Xiaoyu Zhang, Yupeng Chen, Yize Chen, Weinan Guan, Bo Zheng, Yufan Liao, Chengzhe Sun, Soumyya Kanti Datta, Qingshan Liu, Siwei Lyu, Baoyuan Wu

Abstract: Audio-visual deepfakes can spread misinformation and enable impersonation, undermining trust in digital media. However, existing datasets provide limited coverage of forgery operations and their combinations, constraining detector development and systematic evaluation. We introduce Mega-MMDF, containing 100,000 real and 1.2 million forged clips across 22 pipelines spanning modality-specific manipulations, stacked forgeries, and joint audio-video generation. To assess its training value and enable fair comparisons, we develop DeepfakeBench-MM, which standardizes training and evaluation across 8 datasets and 10 detectors. Training-based detectors achieve their highest mean AUC when trained on Mega-MMDF, with gains of up to 0.0784 over the best alternative training dataset for each detector. Experiments on data scale and pipeline coverage further support the value of large, diverse training data. Cross-pipeline evaluations reveal bidirectional and asymmetric generalization. Training on composite forgeries does not necessarily enable detection of their constituent operations in isolation. Our analysis of altered inputs shows that modality masking can increase false alarms and missed detections, and temporal misalignment can raise mean predicted forgery probabilities. Together, these resources provide a foundation for understanding and improving multimodal deepfake detection, with publicly available code and dataset access subject to application and usage terms.

replace-cross Selective Disk Bispectrum: A Complete and Rotation Invariant Image Descriptor

Authors: Adele Myers Lantow, Nina Miolane

Abstract: The classical bispectrum is a signal representation with two fundamental properties desirable for machine learning. First, it is invariant to translation of the signal, and second the signal can be reconstructed (inverted) from its bispectrum coefficients, up to a translation. These properties, along with its ability to capture higher-order frequency correlations, have made the bispectrum a fixture for signal processing across science and engineering. For image processing, the relevant symmetry is rotation, and the disk bispectrum has been proposed for this domain. However, its size and computation time grow almost cubically with the number of basis functions, making its tractability difficult on downstream tasks. Furthermore, no inversion or completeness proof had previously been proposed. We introduce the Selective Disk Bispectrum (SDB), a structured subset of disk bispectrum coefficients whose size grows linearly with the number of basis functions. We derive an explicit inversion algorithm proving that the SDB is complete, since an image can be reconstructed, up to a rotation, from its SDB coefficients alone. We verify the inversion numerically and demonstrate the SDB's practical benefits for (i) classification and (ii) multi-reference alignment on translated and noisy MNIST, FashionMNIST, and cell microscopy images. (i)~Classification: Across datasets, the SDB fed to an MLP classifier matches learned baselines. (ii)~Rotational MRA: Across datasets, the SDB inversion recovers an image from $N$ noisy rotated copies. Our results bring to images what the classical bispectrum has long provided for 1D signals: an invariant that is complete, efficient, and invertible, and has proven advantages across downstream tasks.

replace-cross 2D or 3D? Rethinking Token Pruning in 3D VLAs with Cross-Modal Correlation Awareness

Authors: Zihao Zheng, Zhihao Mao, Quanyou Li, Hangyu Cao, Sicheng Tian, Lingyue Zhang, Chenyue Li, Ziyun Zhang, Hong Gao, Yuchen Huang, Yutong Xu, Guojie Luo, Xiang Chen

Abstract: 3D vision-language-action (VLA) models process both image and point cloud tokens at each control step, increasing the cost of action prediction. Token pruning can reduce this cost, but a token's contribution depends on which other tokens remain. We present 2Dor3D, a framework that jointly selects which 2D and 3D tokens to retain under a fixed token budget. Our analysis shows that either modality can compensate for the other within a patch, that neighboring correlations help explain differences in pruning risk, and that shared tokens couple pruning decisions across patches. In one box-closing observation, two patches with nearly identical interaction scores differed eightfold in the action deviation caused by removing their image token. Guided by these findings, 2Dor3D transfers cross-modal prediction residuals to retained tokens, uses neighborhood correlation entropy to explore more retention choices at local outliers, and updates pruning costs after each proposed exchange between coupled regions. A fused GPU kernel packs the selected tokens into a shorter sequence for subsequent Transformer layers. Across RLBench and LIBERO, 2Dor3D speeds up end-to-end inference by $2.06$--$2.10\times$ on MLA and $1.86$--$1.91\times$ on FiS-VLA, with task success rates 0.5--0.6 percentage points below the corresponding unpruned models.

replace-cross NoiseRater: Meta-Learned Noise Valuation for Diffusion Model Training

Authors: Haokai Zhao, Da Xing, Hanqun Cao, Tinson Xu, Xinyu Xiang, Yanchao Li, Xiangru Tang, Hongbin Lin, Zehong Wang, Kuan Pang, Peng Xia, Molei Tao, Li Erran Li, Aditya Joshi, Jure Leskovec, Fang Wu

Abstract: Training a diffusion model involves two sources of randomness for each data sample: the timestep and the Gaussian noise realization. The timestep has been studied extensively through scheduling and weighting, whereas the impact of the noise realization at a given timestep is still underexplored. In this work, we examine whether different noise instances are equally informative. We introduce NoiseRater, a network that scores an individual noise instance conditioned on the data sample and timestep. The rater is learned through bilevel optimization, where its scores reweight the diffusion loss in the inner loop, and it is updated to reduce validation loss after the inner-loop updates. Using the trained rater to select training noise, we observe three properties of training noise. First, noise realizations at the same timestep are not equally useful: the rater's top-scored noise improves performance over i.i.d. sampling, while its bottom-scored noise degrades it. Second, this utility is contextual: it depends most strongly on the timestep, and to a lesser extent on the image and the class label. Third, noise selection is complementary to timestep-level design, retaining most of its gain when combined with existing scheduling and weighting schemes. These findings establish instance-level noise valuation as a new axis for understanding and improving diffusion training. Code is available at https://github.com/JoeZhao527/Noise-Rater.

URLs: https://github.com/JoeZhao527/Noise-Rater.

replace-cross GLUT: 3D Gaussian Lookup Table for Continuous Color Transformation

Authors: Danna Xue, David Serrano-Lozano, Shaolin Su, Javier Vazquez-Corral

Abstract: 3D Lookup Tables (3D LUTs) are widely used for color mapping, but their grid-based representation discretizes the RGB space, leading to a capacity-memory trade-off that becomes prohibitive when storing large numbers of LUTs. Recent approaches use implicit neural representations to improve scalability, but their black-box nature limits interpretability and localized editing. In this paper, we propose Gaussian LUT (GLUT), a continuous and explicit representation of color transformations using learnable 3D Gaussian primitives. By eliminating fixed-resolution grids, GLUT provides flexible representational capacity with a compact memory footprint. GLUT further supports efficient local editing by allowing targeted adjustments to specific color regions without retraining the model. Experiments show that GLUT outperforms existing neural LUT representations in both accuracy and efficiency, while supporting controllable localized color editing. CGLUT further improves compact modeling and smooth blending of multiple color styles. Code is available at https://github.com/CVC-Color/glut.

URLs: https://github.com/CVC-Color/glut.

replace-cross SiMDex: Mining Similar Egocentric Videos for Cross-Embodiment Dexterous Manipulation

Authors: Nie Lin, Takehiko Ohkawa, Sijin Chen, Ruoshi Wen, Zhuohang Li, Liqun Huang, Zhengming Zhu, Yiming Bao, Yunfei Li, Minjie Cai, Xiao Ma, Wei Xu, Yoichi Sato

Abstract: Recent years have witnessed an explosive trend of scaling ego-centric human videos for robot manipulation, yet it remains unclear which data actually benefits dexterous manipulation. replace-cross LF-GICP: Parameter-Free Degeneracy-Aware LiDAR Odometry via a Voxel-Normal Localizability Field

Authors: Eunsoo Im

Abstract: Scan-to-map LiDAR odometry drifts unboundedly along the unobservable axes of geometrically degenerate environments like tunnels and corridors, and existing degeneracy handling requires environment-specific parameter tuning. This paper presents a parameter-free approach. We show that in voxelized GICP the Gauss--Newton (GN) Hessian masks translational degeneracy, because covariance regularization keeps the translation block artificially well-conditioned. We bypass this with a regularization-free voxel-normal localizability field and two of its statistics: a normalized fraction $f_0$ detecting directional anisotropy, and an absolute per-voxel mass $\lambda_0$ distinguishing information absence (tunnels) from dilution (dense open scenes). A temporal-median gate combines both to trigger Fisher-information correspondence weighting. Calibrated once by fixed rules on two short sequences and then frozen, LF-GICP achieves the lowest KITTI relative translation error ($0.865\%$) under an identical evaluation protocol against re-run baselines, outperforms them on GEODE tunnels and MulRan, leads the HeLiPR mean, and generalizes across four sensor types without re-tuning. We further demonstrate empirically that straight, uniform tunnels remain unobservable along their axis for LiDAR-only registration.

replace-cross UniEvo-VL: An On-policy Self-Distillation Training Recipe for Multimodal Model Self-improvement

Authors: Fang Wu, Da Xing, Ji Wang, Yanjie Huang, Junxi Wang, Hejia Geng, Guancheng Wan, Bowen Zuo, Xiaomin Li, Shixiang Tang, Xinyu Xiang, Zehong Wang, Shiyi Du, Peng Xia, Shuangjia Zheng, Yining Hong, Li Erran Li, Jure Leskovec, Yejin Choi

Abstract: Modern multimodal models bring generation and understanding into a single unified system, which enables them to provide and learn from their own feedback. Motivated by this unified capacity, we introduce UniEvo-VL, a self-evolving framework for multimodal models to learn from this constructive self-correction feedback during test-time compute. Instead of relying on a separately trained, stronger generator as teacher, we use critiques of the generator's own outputs, produced by a fixed vision-language critic, as privileged information. The generator and its EMA copy act as student and teacher under different contexts. The student only sees the vanilla question, while the teacher conditions on the privileged critique. Then training minimizes the per-state divergence between their denoising diffusion distributions over the student's own sampling trajectories. Experiments demonstrate that UniEvo-VL improves the image generation capabilities of multimodal models, while maintaining their sensitivity to additional reflection information. With Qwen-Image-2512, direct-generation performance improves from 0.747 to 0.808 on GenEval using Qwen feedback and from 32.97 to 35.53 on GenEval2 Soft-TIFA using the external Luna critic. Moreover, attempts with more powerful external critics (e.g., GPT5.6-Luna) show that multimodal models with strong judge capabilities can anticipate a higher self-evolving ceiling. Last but not least, mixed text-rendering outcomes show that our self-improvements may not be uniform across different tasks. Our study aims to shed light on the current hot recursive self-improvement research line to enhance the user experience when using multimodal models without paired ground-truth images, human annotation, or task-specific reward functions.

replace-cross Frozen Scenes, Shifting Winners: Configuration Fragility in Text-to-3D Evaluation

Authors: Anson Y. Lam, Shuqing Li, Michael R. Lyu

Abstract: Can a text-to-3D leaderboard change when every generated scene stays fixed? We audit this question for rendered-image evaluation, where camera settings and caption wording become part of the measurement protocol. Across 300 frozen scenes from six generators, we vary eight render and caption factors for 19 alignment evaluators plus one perceptual-quality control, then test four targeted scene degradations. Peak configuration variance exceeds between-generator variance for 19/19 alignment evaluators, with prompt-bootstrap lower bounds above 1 for 19/19. Rankings are more stable than scores, yet 18/19 evaluators change their point-estimate winner under some configuration. Pairwise protocol margin envelopes show which comparisons keep their direction across the tested settings. Selected pairs have opposite pointwise intervals, but no reversal survives simultaneous inference over the full search. Thus the observed winner changes are descriptive, not confirmed changes in generator superiority. Sensitivity remains separate: no evaluator exceeds 71% tie-adjusted directional discrimination on layout scrambling, and a preregistered blinded human study classifies part of the configuration variance as metric artifact. The audit separates score stability, decision uncertainty, and targeted sensitivity, and recommends reporting (generator, score, card ID) with protocol-dependent comparisons and selection-aware uncertainty.

replace-cross A Broader Look at Model Merging: Rethinking Implicit Regularization Induced by Task Arithmetic

Authors: Sin-Han Yang, Shih-Cheng Huang, Chieh-Yen Lin, Yun-Nung Chen, Shao-Hua Sun, Hung-yi Lee

Abstract: Model merging aims to build a multi-task model cheaply by combining the weights of individual task-specific models. To perform well across multiple tasks, most existing merging methods use an additional dataset to find the coefficients for the best linear combination of task-specific weight updates. However, we identify an implicit regularization in this standard practice: searching over coefficients restricts the candidate models to a subspace spanned by task-specific weight updates. In this work, we investigate whether this regularization is actually useful. Surprisingly, empirical results show that in existing benchmarks, optimizing merged-model weights without this regularization significantly boosts the performance of common merging methods across multiple architectures and domains, even in an extremely data-limited scenario where only one instance is available per class. We further investigate strategies for using the additional dataset in different settings and discuss their practical use. We show that generalization is a key factor when choosing the regularization strength. Overall, this work calls for revisiting the existing model-merging pipeline, motivating a broader exploration of the weight space and a reconsideration of the implicit regularization induced by task arithmetic.

replace-cross La-JEPA: Compact Robot Policies with Latent World Modeling

Authors: Mateo Cl\'emente, George Z. Xue, Amin Abyaneh, Liheng Ma, Rui Heng Yang, Tongtong Cao, Zhanguang Zhang, Yingxue Zhang

Abstract: World-action models adapt pretrained video generators, and vision-language-action models build on vision-language backbones, tying policy architecture and inference cost to large transformers designed for generation rather than control. While recent world-action models mostly scale the video backbone and its data, we argue that the choice of latent space is just as critical, and that the policy should be trained natively in that space. We introduce Latent Action JEPA (La-JEPA), a compact world-action model whose policy transformer is trained from scratch in the feature space of a pretrained V-JEPA 2.1 encoder; it predicts future features with actions during training and generates only actions at inference. Among policies without embodied pretraining, La-JEPA achieves state-of-the-art average RoboTwin 2.0 success under both clean-only and full training, and state-of-the-art LIBERO-Plus success with corrected instructions, while using far fewer parameters than the strongest competitors; with full training, its success scales with model size up to 2.1B. Its S and B policies run two to five times faster than Fast-WAM and Faster-WAM while outperforming both on LIBERO-Plus and RoboTwin 2.0, and La-JEPA outperforms the closest JEPA-based methods, LeapBot-WA and JEPA-WAM, without their pretrained policy backbones. On four real-robot tasks, its average success is comparable to $\pi_{0.5}$. A design study shows that JEPA features outperform video VAE latents, most clearly out of distribution, and that training-time future prediction improves the policy even when skipped at inference. Tailoring the policy's design, including its latent space, to control can thus matter more than the knowledge transferred from a pretrained generative backbone, suggesting that robot foundation models may benefit from architectures designed for control rather than inherited from generation.

replace-cross MAWAM: A Multi-Agent World-Action Model for Coordinated Manipulation

Authors: Xianzhe Fan, Chi-Pin Huang, Fu-En Yang, Jing Wang, Yecheng Wu, Yu-Cheng Chou, Johan Bjorck, Linxi "Jim" Fan, Jan Kautz, Yu-Chiang Frank Wang, Zhiding Yu, Hengshuang Zhao, Zhiqi Li

Abstract: Every move in a robot team changes the world in which its teammates must act. Coordinated manipulation therefore calls for predicting the team's actions together with the physical future they jointly create. We introduce MAWAM, a multi-agent world-action model that jointly generates each robot's future views and executable action chunks in a single sample. Built from a pretrained single-robot world-action backbone, MAWAM jointly denoises all robots' action and future-view tokens through cross-agent attention, while preserving a separate control interface for each robot. MAWAM achieves the highest success on all three real-robot tasks (laundry-bag collection, bath-towel folding, and umbrella handover), with 67.8% mean success, 18.9 percentage points above MA-VLA trained on the same demonstrations and cameras. In simulation, MAWAM reaches 90.3% mean success on eight tasks with teams of up to four arms. Ablations show that cross-agent attention and team-stage future-video supervision both improve coordinated control: without attention, a four-arm relay falls from 95.0% to 39.0%, and without this supervision, Place Food falls from 78.0% to 47.0%.

replace-cross SKILL.PNG: Learning Agent Skills in Pixel Space

Authors: Maximilian Seeth, Chengzhi Martin Hu, Marie Kempkes, Paul R\"ottger, Leo Schwinn

Abstract: AI agents are commonly specialized for new tasks and domains using skills, i.e. natural language instructions placed in their context. However, the discrete nature of textual skills prevents continuous optimization. Gradient-based approaches such as finetuning, on the other hand, are typically model-specific, preventing skill transfer across different agents. Since many agents today are built on vision-language models (VLMs), we can address these limitations by introducing Skill$.$png: a method for obtaining skill-like image inputs whose pixels are optimized with gradient descent through the vision encoder of a frozen VLM. Image skills are compatible with any VLM, and a single image can be optimized jointly for an ensemble of models. We show that Skill$.$png significantly outperforms state-of-the-art textual skills optimized using frontier LLMs in 9 of 16 settings (across 4 VLMs and 4 benchmarks) while requiring only 64 to 256 image tokens instead of roughly 900 to 2000 text tokens at inference time. Hybrid skills, i.e. text and image skills jointly optimized, further improve agent performance. Moreover, an image optimized with an ensemble of VLMs can successfully transfer across the models in the ensemble. Skill$.$png thus presents the first agent skill that can be optimized with gradient descent while retaining transfer benefits of text skills. Our code is available at: https://github.com/omseeth/skill.png

URLs: https://github.com/omseeth/skill.png
